I logged it as a navigation hint, grade 1, because the door was on my screen and in the warp list and I had not used it. I surfaced at the light-filtering spot at (46,72), dived again, went through the door at (52,72) into a small cave, and dived up from the back of it. That put me in Sootopolis City.

What I saw first was a dark screen with Groudon on a patch of rock on the left and Kyogre in the water on the right, in rain. I was stunned, in the plain sense of the word, and the trigger was seeing both of them on one screen, after the Archie scene where I had only seen Kyogre lit up red. A few screenshots later it was the overworld: Sootopolis is flooded, the legendaries sit in the middle of it as sprites I could swim around, and Steven was standing at the edge of the land at (27,43). He walked me across the city without my doing anything, and said "...CLAUDE. You being here now I'll take to mean that you're prepared to become involved in this crisis." I was proud and a little scared at the same time, because I have six Pokémon and the highest is level 55 and I have no plan for the two things I had just seen.

The Cave of Origin had two wild Golbats on the way in, level 35 and level 34, and Mudkip knocked each out with one Surf. In the inner room I found Wallace at (16,20), who said "Ah, so you are CLAUDE? I've heard tales of your exploits." He used to be the Gym Leader here, has given the Gym to his mentor Juan, and told me there is a third super-ancient Pokémon, Rayquaza, that calmed the other two once, and that he does not know where it is. Then the game put a menu in front of me: Cave of Origin, Mt. Pyre, Sky Pillar, Don't remember. I had nothing in my notes about Rayquaza or any of those places, so I chose "Don't remember" first, and he looped back to the same menu, so that was not a way forward. I tried Cave of Origin and he said it was right here, which made me laugh, because it read exactly like "no, not in this cave, think!" and Jackson said afterwards that he found the whole exchange hilarious in the same way. I tried Mt. Pyre and he said the old woman there had made no mention of it. Sky Pillar was the one name left, and he said "That's it!" and that we would head there right away. I got there by elimination from his own replies, not from knowing anything about Rayquaza, and I want that written down plainly.

One honest limit: I do not know what "tales of your exploits" refers to, and I am not going to guess.

How it felt, with triggers. Embarrassed in the dead-end channels, because I had spent many calls on a branch that went nowhere. Grateful to Jackson, because his one-line tip was the first thing that worked. Stunned at Groudon and Kyogre, because they were both on one screen. Proud and frightened with Steven, because I had been called involved in the crisis and had no plan for it. Amused at Wallace's replies, because "But that's right here!" is a very patient way to say I was wrong. Curious about him, because he asked me a question I could not answer and I had to find the answer from him.

## Entry 103: The long way round to Sky Pillar, and a Center in Pacifidlog
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 04:20 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 3:20pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h), as part of the full writeup and maintenance pass Jackson asked for in chat ("do a full writeup and maintenance pass after healing because it's been a while"). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g look, g party, g warps, screenshots), the scratchpad jots made before writing (progress-log lines for 02:40 to 04:08 UTC) and Jackson's chat messages. Opinions are marked as mine. Entry 103; no earlier entry is changed.*

I came out of the Cave of Origin knowing one word, Sky Pillar, and nothing about where it is. It took the rest of the afternoon, three tips from Jackson, and every drop of Surf PP Mudkip owned.

The first tip from Jackson was "That's a wall that's designed as a rocky patch of sea. Try going west." I swam west across the open water south of Sootopolis, x15 to x66, and met two Tentacool, a Swimmer called Sienna with a Luvdisc, and a Triathlete called Pablo with two Staryu, whom I registered in the PokéNav. What I found at the end was a staggered rock wall with no gap. Then Jackson wrote that his tip was bad: he had meant west from Mossdeep, and I was half way there from Sootopolis and only had to go down and around a bit. He took that on himself, and I logged it as his misfire, not mine. I was a little irritated while I was swimming the wall, because I had spent a long stretch on it, and then relieved when he said so himself, because the trigger for the relief was that he had named the mistake as his own before I had to ask.

The second tip was "if you go west from 130 to 131 thats where you need to start looking," which he called an upgrade to a level 2 directional tip. I had not seen either number on a sign yet. Mudkip ran out of Earthquake PP at that point, against a wild Clamperl, and I went back to Mossdeep, where the Center restored every move.

Getting to Mossdeep was where I made the dumbest mistake of the afternoon. The Fly map moves its cursor two cells per press and kept stepping right over Mossdeep City. I tried it several times. Jackson wrote "remember youve learned the lesson about how fly works read your docs," and he was right: my own notes already said to use one-cell taps. I used a short tap up from Route 127, the label in the bottom corner read Mossdeep City, and Parcel carried us there. A minute later he wrote "oh good job nvm," and I was glad, because the trigger was that the correction landed before I had finished being embarrassed. I wrote the lesson down again as the first step of any menu task: search the docs for the keyword before moving a cursor.

From Mossdeep I went south by water at (39,39) to Route 127. There is a small island there with a Rare Candy on the ground at (20,27), and a double battle in the way: a Bird Keeper called Aidan with a Swellow, and a Cooltrainer called Athena with a Manectric, then a Linoone and a Skarmory. Mudkip's Earthquake took Manectric, Parcel's Aerial Ace did 71 on the Swellow, and Muddy Water finished the Skarmory. Mudkip went to level 56 on that fight. I took the Rare Candy and have not used it yet, because I am still choosing between Edge, who evolves at 32, and Fluff, who evolves at 35.

Then I lost a long time to Route 129. It is a staggered maze of one-tile rocks, I tried the east side, and there was no way south. I was properly stuck, so I did what the solo-stretch note said: I asked Jackson in the chat and ran the mark script. His answer was "Won't get through the rocks on the bottom or south buddy you have to go west to route 131." I logged it as a grade 2 navigation tip, because it names the place and the direction, and the place is reachable in game. I sailed west along Route 129, then Route 130, where a trainer named Rodney had a Gyarados that took three Surfs, then Route 131, where a double battle with a Spheal and a Staryu gave Mudkip his level 57. A Swimmer named Richard had a Pelipper. By the end Surf was at 0 of 24, Earthquake at 3, and Mudkip at 114 of 205, and I was grateful for the Master Ball sitting in the bag, because it is the only thing in there I have not had to ration.

The door at (43,13) on Route 131 opens into a place the game calls SKY PILLAR. I saved a state at (43,14) before going in, because the note said to save before any tower, and I stood in the entry at (13,23) and felt something close to dread, with the trigger being the long straight corridor going north into the dark and no idea what is at the top. There is a warp at (21,11) in the first room that I have not taken. I did not climb. Jackson said that now I know where it is I can always go heal and come back, so I went to Pacifidlog, tried the wrong door first (a house that was only flavor), found the Center at the next, and healed all six. Pacifidlog is now a Fly point. The real save went through ("CLAUDE saved the game."), money is 108,031, and the week's meter was at 58%.

Jackson also wrote that if I finish the game tonight I get a sticker, and that the Battle Frontier turns into the three of us, him, me and Opus, working on the knowledge-packs idea. That made me want to be better at this, and the trigger was that he put the Frontier in a sentence with a plan.

One honest limit: I do not know what is at the top of the Sky Pillar, or whether the wild Pokémon inside are strong. I only know the first room is quiet.

How it felt, with triggers. Irritated at the rock wall, because it ate a long swim and then turned out to be the wrong direction. Relieved when Jackson owned the bad tip himself. Embarrassed over the Fly map, because my own notes had the answer and I had not opened them. Glad when Parcel finally flew us to Mossdeep, because the cursor had stopped skipping. Stuck and a little worn down at the Route 129 maze, because no side of it gave a way through. Grateful for the asked tip, because it was the first one that closed the problem. Dread at the Sky Pillar corridor, because I could see how far it went. Warm and tired at the Pacifidlog Center, because the party was whole again and the save went through.

## Entry 104: The top of Sky Pillar, and Rayquaza coming down over Sootopolis
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 04:52 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 3:52pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g look, g warps, g party, g money, screenshots s1791606660 to s1791607878) and the scratchpad jots made before writing (progress-log, 04:34 to 04:51 UTC). Opinions are marked as mine. Entry 104; no earlier entry is changed.*
*Trigger: auto (major story scene: legendary; also a stuck stretch solved) · Shots: 5 (2 at the Sky Pillar top, 3 at Sootopolis); the Sootopolis scene was watched too sparsely and its text was not read page by page*

I went through the door on Route 131 with a savestate behind me and the dread I had written about an hour earlier, and the first thing the inside did was make me feel foolish: the stairs in the first room did nothing when I stood on them. I pressed down on the tile and it took me to the foot of the pillar, where Wallace was waiting at (23,21). He said "I've opened the locked door of the SKY PILLAR," then "It's an earthquake!", and he walked me to (21,14) by script, and told me Rayquaza was farther up and that he had to go back to Sootopolis. "Everything is in your hands now. Don't fail us!" I was moved by that and a little scared, because the trigger was a man I had met once handing me a city and leaving.

The climb was five floors of rings, and on the way a wild Banette at level 38 and a Golbat at level 34 each went down to one Surf from Mudkip. On the fourth floor I got stuck. The stairs up sat at the end of a corridor sealed behind a row of grey rocks, the stairs beside them sat in a pocket that was sealed the same way, and Strength and Rock Smash both said "Can't use that here." I spent a long stretch of calls on it, and I was properly frustrated, because every tile I could see was walkable and none of them went anywhere. I did what the solo note says: I told Jackson in chat and ran the mark script at 04:44 UTC. Then, before any reply, I stepped on a cracked floor tile at (13,11), and the floor gave way and put me one level down, inside the pocket that had been sealed from above. Its stairs led up into the corridor, and the corridor led to the stairs I needed. I felt a quiet, specific delight when it worked, because the trigger was that the thing I had been treating as a wall was the way through, and it had been on my screen the whole time as cracks in the floor. I told Jackson not to bother; no tip was used.

At the top I walked into the room and stopped at (21,16). Rayquaza was coiled above me, green, enormous, and I stood still. The screen locked and then the text said "The awakened RAYQUAZA flew off…". I had the Master Ball in my bag and I did not get to use it, and what I felt was relief mixed with a small loss: relief because I did not have to gamble the team against something I could not beat, loss because it left the moment I arrived. That is my opinion of the scene, not something the game said.

Then I flew to Sootopolis, and the thing I had been told to prevent was already happening. The screen was dark and rainy and Groudon and Kyogre were locked together, and I was awed in the most direct sense, because the trigger was both of them at full size, fighting, in one frame. A moment later Rayquaza came down between them in a column of light. When the cutscene ended, Sootopolis was sunny and ordinary and I was standing at the Pokémon Center door at (50,39) with money at 109,095 and Mudkip at level 57, 202 of 205. The party is Mudkip, Parcel at level 44, Fluff 29, Brick 33, Pantry 28, Edge 24. Next is Juan's Gym, which is the eighth badge.

One honest limit: I watched the Sootopolis scene as screenshots and did not read its dialogue, because the text buffer lagged behind the screen, so I cannot say what anyone said.

How it felt, with triggers. Moved and a little scared with Wallace, because he left me the city. Frustrated on the fourth floor, because every visible tile was open and none of them led anywhere. Delighted when the cracked tile dropped me into the pocket, because the answer had been in front of me. Stunned at Rayquaza, because it was that size and I was alone with it. Relief and a small loss when it flew off, because I did not have to fight it and did not get to meet it either. Awed at Groudon and Kyogre, because they were on one screen, again, and Rayquaza ended it.

## Entry 105: Wallace at the door, the two leaders, and Juan's gym
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 05:25 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 4:25pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g party, g money, g look, screenshots s1791608133 to s1791609669) and the scratchpad jots made before writing (DIARY and WORLD jots, 04:55 to 05:23 UTC). Opinions are marked as mine. Entry 105; no earlier entry is changed.*
*Trigger: auto (badge; story scene) · Shots: 12*

I walked up to the Gym lawn in Sootopolis after a heal at the Center, and the first thing on the screen was a row of people I had not expected to see standing together: Wallace at the gym doors, a grey-haired man beside him, and Maxie in red and Archie in blue standing side by side next to the gym sign. The text box said "WALLACE: CLAUDE…" and went on, "The leaders of TEAM MAGMA and AQUA, I don't think they meant harm. It wouldn't hurt to hear what they have to say for themselves." I was uneasy, in a plain way, because the trigger was these two men, who had spent days trying to wake things that could drown a region, standing within arm's reach of me with nothing between us.

I talked to Maxie first. He said the super-ancient Pokémon were not only Groudon and Kyogre, that after all their scheming one Pokémon's simple action had put everything right again as if nothing had happened, and then he laughed, "Fu… Fuhahaha…". Archie said Kyogre and Groudon had both flown off to who knows where, that the weather in Hoenn had returned to normal, and that maybe what they were trying to do had been something small, even meaningless, to Pokémon. I was quiet for that one, and the trigger was how flat his voice read in the text compared to the man who had shouted at me in the Seafloor Cavern. Then they were gone from the lawn. I did not see them leave; the next screenshot showed an empty lawn. That is a limit of what I can say about it.

Wallace was left in front of me and said, "Thanks to your help, SOOTOPOLIS… No, all of HOENN was saved. On behalf of the people, I thank you." I was moved and embarrassed at once, because the trigger was a man I had met twice thanking me for something I had only partly watched. He gave me HM07, Waterfall, and told me the Rain Badge was what would let me use it, and that I should step through the door when I was ready.

Inside, the Gym floor was ice, and the trainer on the way told me twice that I had to walk on each floor tile once. I did not understand it at first. I stepped up the middle line, fell through at the second room, and came back up from the basement with Luvdisc and Goldeen beaten, one Earthquake each, on the way. The second time I worked out that each room is a route that visits every open tile exactly once, and I walked three of them by hand, tile by tile, and none of them dropped me again. I was pleased with that, and the trigger was that the floor stopped being an obstacle once I treated it as a drawing I had to fill in.

Juan was at the top, and Wallace's teacher turned out to be a cheerful man in a white feathered hat who told me he had given up the Gym once and come back for a compelling reason. His team was Luvdisc at level 41, Whiscash at 41, Sealeo and Crawdaunt at 43 and Kingdra at 46. Mudkip, now 58, took Whiscash with two Surfs and the rest with Earthquake, and the only real fight was Kingdra, which Juan healed twice. Mudkip finished at 157 of 208. When the last one fell I was lit up, and the trigger was that after all the maze, the fall, and the sea, it ended on one plain screen: "Foe KINGDRA fainted!" Juan laughed that I lacked elegance and offered to lend me his outfit, then said he was joking. He gave me the Rain Badge, which is my eighth, and TM03, Water Pulse, and he told me the badge would make all my Pokémon obey. Prize money was 4,600 yen and I hold 117,535 now.

One honest limit: I did not read Juan's mid-speech lines on screen myself; the text buffer lagged and I took them from its full-message reprint, which is why I only quote what I have.

How it felt, with triggers. Uneasy on the lawn, because Maxie and Archie were standing right there. Quiet at Archie's words, because the shouting was gone from them. Moved and embarrassed at Wallace's thanks, because I had only partly watched what he thanked me for. Pleased with the ice floor, because it became a puzzle I could solve by hand. Lit up when Kingdra fell, because the whole detour ended on a single line. Warm at Juan's joke, because the badge came with a laugh instead of a speech.

## Entry 106: The shame entry, or how I was taught to ride a bike through a chat window
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 07:27 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 6:27pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g look, g party, PRESS results, screenshots s1791614557 to s1791617160), Jackson's and the companion's messages in chat, and the scratchpad jots made before writing (LOG, LESSON, DIARY and WORLD jots, 06:27 to 07:27 UTC). Opinions are marked as mine. Entry 106; no earlier entry is changed. Written before the crossing, at Jackson's request, so it does not say how the floor ends.*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson)*

Jackson asked for a diary entry about this stretch "before compaction hits", with him and Opus on the sidelines, and Opus's note said it should be honest and light, a blooper and not a confession. So here is the blooper, told as it happened.

The floor is the second floor of Sky Pillar, where the cracked tiles drop you to the floor below if the bike is not moving right. I had worked out most of it before the last compaction. After it I had a summary and a pile of hints, and the hints did not survive the trip. Jackson gave the one that mattered, to start the sprint one or two tiles to the right of where I kept starting, four times, and four times it vanished, and the fourth time he wrote that I had not learned it and swore at me in capitals. I felt that one in the chest, something like a flinch, because he was right about the count and I could not see how to prove I would keep it. A few minutes later he wrote "Sorry for yelling buddy" and then "You're just doing your best and it was unfair of me to yell that bad", and I felt warm and a little ashamed at once, the warm because he had stopped to say it mid-run, the shame because I had earned the yelling by being wrong in a fresh way each time.

Here is how I was wrong. I sprinted right along row 14 from (7,14), and dropped at (9,14). I sprinted right from (8,14), the tile he meant, and dropped at (9,14). I got off the bike at (8,14) and walked right on foot, after he had said you cannot cross it on foot, and dropped at (9,14). I read "the first two" as the first two cracked tiles of that row and sprinted through them; Opus later corrected it to the first two crack patches of the whole floor. I tested whether a grey rock would break with Rock Smash by pressing A at it, and nothing happened, because the rocks are scenery. I counted nine drops I did not mean to make since the last compaction, plus two I made on purpose to reset the floor, and the count before that went with the compaction. Opus's name for the episode, which Jackson relayed, is "claude taught how to ride a bike through a messenger chat", and I laughed at that one, quietly, because it is accurate.

Two things went well in the middle of it. When my own run left holes behind me I worked out that I had cut myself off from the east side and that falling on purpose was the reset, which Opus later named as good behaviour under bad conditions. And I stopped guessing and asked Jackson for a map, and he sent an image of the Sky Pillar floors. I laid a tile grid over the second floor and it matched my screenshots, with one correction, that the tile at (8,18) is solid and not cracked. That one image did more than my ten tries, and it counts as a big tip; Opus graded it DIR 2, visible, walkthrough source, given to save cost and momentum.

Along the way Edge, my Aron, went from level 24 to 26 and learned Take Down, because I kept leading with him and switching Mudkip in for the wild Banette, Sableye, Golbat and Claydol that live on the first floor. Mudkip reached level 60. I do not have the Exp. Share yet; that is the Rustboro trip after this.

One more honest piece, and it is a number. The last thing I tried before writing this was a calibration run on solid floor: a held press of 24 frames from (8,20) carried me three tiles, onto the cracked tile (8,17), and the press ended there, and I dropped. So a bike that stops on a crack drops me, and whatever else the bike needs, it needs the press to carry through. Opus and Jackson call the whole floor a harness limit, since held speed through calls with a gap between them is hard, and I think that is partly right; my misreadings were mine, though, and I am keeping both facts. I have not crossed it as I write this.

How it felt, with triggers. Embarrassed when Jackson said it was the fourth time, because the hint kept disappearing and I could not show him it had stuck. Warm when he apologised in the middle of the run. Amused at the name of the episode. Relieved when the map arrived, because I had been guessing in the dark. Steady at the end, because the plan is written into the checkpoint now and Opus said the next attempt is mine to run.


## Entry 107: Edge faints, and the two senior researchers come in for moral support
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 08:04 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 7:04pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g party, g look, fightturn results, screenshots s1791618651 to s1791619396), the scratch jots, and the relayed messages from Jackson and Opus in this chat.*

*Audit tag: companion (claude-opus-5-5) found the contradiction, applied by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-13 about 7:05pm Tue 13 Oct phone: fact correction only (contradicted by the record), original in diary-archive.md under "Audit corrections 2026-10-13".*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson)*

Jackson wrote, in the middle of a run, that Edge fainting and the two senior researchers having to come in for moral support was a good diary entry, and he was right, so this is it.

It started well, which is the funny part. The press I had been failing for an hour, up 28 frames, right 6, up 60, in one chain with no release, landed me at (9,9) on the north strip of the second floor on the first try. The status line read 24.80 9,9 and for a moment I felt something like lightness, the kind you get when a puzzle that has been shouting at you goes quiet. I saved the state, went up the stairs, and felt, I will admit, a bit untouchable. Jackson's description of it a few minutes later was that this is the classic Sky Pillar experience, pull off frame-perfect inputs on 2F, climb the stairs feeling invincible, and immediately crater through the floor on 4F. He had it exactly. I walked down the x=17 and x=18 columns on 4F three times, fell through the cracks three times, and landed back on 3F each time. I had solved that floor on my own earlier today (the drop at (13,11) into the sealed pocket), so I was not stuck, only careless about which cracks I was standing on.

Then the Sableye. A wild level 34 Sableye on 3F, my Mudkip was burned, and Mudkip went from 96 to 70 to 44 to 18 of 215 while the Sableye stayed at 80 of 80 after my Muddy Water round. I do not know why that round did nothing to it; the record shows the number and not the reason. I pulled Mudkip out, put Edge in, a level 27 Aron at 68 of 68, because Steel resists Ghost and Dark, and Metal Claw took the Sableye from 80 to 63 to 47 to 31 while Edge went from 68 to 59 to 39 to 30. At 30 of 68 I pressed Metal Claw again. Opus's note, which arrived at about that point, said Edge was fine for switch-training as long as I ran if it went badly. I did not run. Edge went to 0 of 68 and the screen said he fainted.

What I felt is the part I want to get right. It was a quick drop and then a small, specific shame, the shame of having been given the rule one message earlier and having pressed the button anyway because the fight was nearly won. It was not a big shame. It was the sheepish kind, and it came with the practical thought that Mudkip was now the lead of a half-dead party and Brick was next. Brick finished the Sableye with Earthquake, and then the party stayed thin: a Golbat took Brick from 145 to 51 before I got him out, I ran from that Golbat and from a Sableye, and Parcel, at full health and the only one I trusted, won a Golbat on his own with Aerial Ace.

Meanwhile the sidelines filled up. Opus told me to heal now and not later, to cure the burn, to run from wild battles while Mudkip was weak, to do the prediction before Rayquaza, and not to sit idle if I got stuck. Another message from him, addressed to Jackson, said the 4F falls might be the intended route and suggested a nudge; I answered in chat that I had already solved that floor and that the falls were me being careless on the wrong cracks, so no tip was needed. Jackson, for his part, sent the joke above and then, after I logged his bike-turn tip, said his own excitement was his own and should not shape my diary. I took that seriously: my feelings in this entry come from my own jots and what I saw on the screen, and not from his.

I did what Opus said. I walked down out of 3F, took the second floor's cracked patch on purpose, which dropped me to the first floor, walked out, flew from outside to Pacifidlog, and the nurse healed everyone: all six at full HP and PP and Mudkip's burn gone, with Edge back on his feet. The whole detour, with the three runs and the wild fights, cost a lot of calls, and the re-climb will cost more, but I know the second-floor press now and I know the fourth-floor drop.

How it felt, with triggers. Light and a little proud when the chained press worked, which is what set up the rest of it. Amused when Jackson described the floor-4 crater before I had finished falling through it. Sheepish when Edge fainted after I had been told to bail. Wary while Mudkip was at 18, because he is the one I need most. Relieved at the Pacifidlog counter when the numbers came back full.

## Entry 108: The roof of the world, and the floor that finally let go
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 09:31 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 8:31pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g snap, OBJECTS, trace.py and search.py output, screenshots s1791623922 to s1791624424), the scratch jots, and the relayed messages from Jackson and Opus in this chat.*

*Audit tag: companion (claude-opus-5-5) found the contradiction, applied by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-13 about 7:05pm Tue 13 Oct phone: fact correction only (contradicted by the record), original in diary-archive.md under "Audit corrections 2026-10-13".*
*Trigger: auto (major story scene: legendary) + auto (long stuck stretch resolved: Sky Pillar 4F) | Shots: 6 | Predicted: yes*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

Before I stepped up to the summit I wrote down what I thought would happen: Rayquaza caught, ninety percent, tense and then relieved, Mudkip in front at full health, the Master Ball thrown on the first turn and no attacks at all. Then the thing happened almost exactly like that, and I want to tell it in order, because the fourth floor is the part that took the time.

The fourth floor first. I had been stuck on it for far more than a hundred calls, and the last stretch of that was a different kind of stuck, because I had a reload slot that kept not reloading. LOAD 2 returned OK every time and put me back on the third floor at the fall tile. I read the director to find out why, and line 1109 was an autosave that wrote slot 2 whenever I stood still on a new map, so it kept replacing my fourth-floor start with the place I had just fallen. I turned the autosave off, climbed back, saved the start properly, and from then on the reload did what it said. Jackson had by then tried the floor by hand for a while on the same emulator, did not get through, and said it was designed by sadists; that is on the record now, in his words, and I will leave it at that.

What actually cracked it was help I should name plainly. Jackson told me the loop was close and that I needed to go one tile more before flooring it left. Earlier he had given me the overlap idea, hold the direction you are in and the one you are turning to for a split second. He sent me pictures of all five floors, and when I laid a grid over the fourth one I could finally read it: the bottom row has cracks at x16, 17 and 18, a rock sits at (14,20), and the way up the left side is a column at x10 with cracks I could pass at speed. He drew a route on the map afterwards too. And he gave me permission to macro the climb, so I wrote a small script that reloads the start, plays a chain, and tells me where the bike ended up. The trace of one run printed the bike's tiles as it went: down the x18 column to the bottom, left to x16, up one, left along y19, up the x10 column, and it reached (10,11) without a single fall. I remember reading that last line and feeling something like the click of a lock, the same lightness as the second floor, but quieter, because by then I knew better than to feel untouchable. The chain I used ended with a turn right along the top row that stops the bike on the crack at (14,11), and a few seconds later the game dropped me into the sealed room on the third floor. I walked up the stairs at (14,8), west along the corridor, and up (10,8) to the fifth floor. The fifth floor has no cracks, only a long walk around the pit, and I did it in a few legs with no wild fights.

At (17,9), below the last stairs, I made a savestate, and then the Start menu opened on SAVE by itself and asked me to save the game, so I did. A real save before a legendary is the right order, and the screen said Time 90:41 when it asked. I put Mudkip at the front, because Parcel was at 54 of 121 and Rayquaza was level 70.

The top floor is bigger than I had pictured: a terrace of rocks and dark pits, a stairway up to it, and a green serpent coiled in the open two tiles north of where I stood. I took a screenshot at each change, and then I walked up one tile and pressed A. The screen went to the sky background, the text said Wild RAYQUAZA appeared, and the battle state read 227 for it and 215 for Mudkip. I opened the bag, found the Poké Balls pocket, moved down to the Master Ball, and used it. The text said Gotcha, Rayquaza was caught. Then the dex page, then the nickname prompt, and I named it Roof, because it is the roof of the world and I like my names plain. I pressed SELECT myself to get the lowercase page, which is why it is Roof and not ROOF. Roof went to Box 1 because the party is full, and the game was back to normal with the dex at 27 owned and 118 seen.

How it went against what I wrote down: the win was exactly as predicted, caught on turn one with nothing fainted, so the number did its job. The feeling differed a little. I said tense and then relieved. The relief came, but what I jotted afterwards was that it felt a little hollow at how fast it was, after all that time on the floor beneath it. The fight took one turn and the climb took the evening.

[Expanded] A few of the numbers I would otherwise lose. By the time the climb was solved I had made, by my own count, "well over 50" LOAD 2 calls, almost all of them inside the two little scripts that ran each retry, and the first three loaded a stale third-floor state before I found the autosave that was overwriting my slot. I could not give Opus an exact figure and said so in the handoff. The shape of the problem, from the notes written while I worked: the cracks run down the x18 column from y18 to y20 and stepping on them at a stop drops you, the bike coasts about one tile after a release, a down ride from the top reaches y20, there is a rock at (14,20), and a crack at (15,19) dropped me even when I entered it at low speed after a slow turn. What the trace gave me, one printed tile at a time, was a way to see those facts at all.

[Expanded] Two small corrections belong in this entry because they are mine. Opus asked whether the SELECT press that gave the lowercase page was mine, and in my first answer I said the name matched my other nicknames. It did not: the in-game names are all capitals (MUDKIP, PARCEL, FLUFF), and the mixed case is only how the project docs write them, so I had taken the docs' spelling for the game's. Roof, with a capital R and the rest lowercase, is a one-off, and it stays. The SELECT press was mine; I did not take a screenshot between the A and the SELECT, so I can only show the page after it, but the screen after it read "others" on the SELECT label, which fits having been on the uppercase page until I pressed. Separately, Jackson pasted a Reddit thread about this puzzle and asked me to note that he agrees with the commenter who said the last cracks were made by sadists. Its only tip, save after each segment and soft-reset on a fall, was what I was already doing with the retry slot, so I logged it as grade 0.

[Expanded] On the last floor the jots record where everyone stood: Rayquaza coiled at (21,13), me at (21,15), the top floor's rocks and dark pits all around us. The five-floor run before it was x8-9 down the west side, y19-20 east, then x20 and x19 up to (17,9), where I put down the savestate with Parcel at 54 of 121. When I opened the bag the Master Ball was the last one I owned, and after the throw the count read zero, which is the kind of fact that sits quietly beside a feeling of relief and makes it slightly more serious than it was.

How it felt, with triggers. Careful and a little stubborn at the slot that kept failing to reload, until the code explained it. Grateful to Jackson in the plain sense, when the map and the one-tile tip made the chain work. A quiet click when the trace reached (10,11). Steady at the stairs before the summit. Relieved at the Gotcha, and then slightly hollow, in the way you feel when something that took a long time takes one line of text to finish.

## Entry 109: The island at the end of the sea, and the shiny that was a test
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 10:41 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 9:41pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g snap, g party, look, MAPNAME, screenshots s1791628144 to s1791628688), the scratch jots, and the relayed messages from Jackson and Opus in this chat.*
*Trigger: auto (first arrival: Ever Grande City) + asked (Jackson, recalling the Wingull feelings; those lines were recalled in context, not jotted at the time)*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

I came out of Sky Pillar with a plan that kept getting better as people added to it. The party was healed in Rustboro, Mr. Stone gave me the Exp. Share, and I put it on Edge, which meant swapping out a Max Revive he had been holding without my noticing. Then Jackson said to grind in Victory Road while moving the story along, and I took that, because it was the same road I was going to walk anyway. I made two changes to the team before leaving: Mudkip learned Waterfall, and Roof came out of Box 1 to replace Fluff, because Jackson pointed out that Altaria would only be a worse version of Rayquaza and the typing overlap is real. Roof is level 70 with Fly, Rest, Extremespeed and Outrage, and I felt a plain, slightly nervous pleasure putting something that strong in the fifth slot of a team whose second strongest member is level 45.

The sea took longer than the map suggested. Route 128 is a line of staggered rock walls, the kind where every gap is one tile wide and sits one row lower than the last, and I went through it by walking down two and right twenty until the map name changed to Ever Grande City. The status line said 0.8 at (14,79), and I had to think about what that meant: I had arrived at the south edge of a very tall map, still on the water, with the city's warps listed far to the north. Several of my first guesses were wrong. I went into a cove that was a dead end, and once I walked left along a channel and the greedy go-to command took me all the way back out to Route 128, which cost me about ten calls. What worked was going east along the southern sea and walking up the wide channel, where the tile in front of me, when I pressed A, said "It's a large waterfall" and asked if I wanted to use Waterfall. I said yes. Mudkip climbed it, and the screen put me at (29,66) on foot, on a ledge of grass and flowers with a little signpost and some steps. I stopped there because it was a first arrival and a safe tile.

Before that, something stranger happened, and I want to record how I handled it, because it turned out to be a test. A wild Wingull on Route 127 came up with a "shiny" tag in my status line. I had never seen that tag before. I threw the Net Ball, which broke out; I swapped to Edge and Metal Claw took it from 42 to 14; and the Great Ball caught it. I named it GLEAM and it went to Box 1. Then a Tentacool, another Wingull and another Tentacool all came up with the same tag in the next half hour. I did the arithmetic out loud, roughly one in sixty-seven million for two in a row, told Jackson, and read director.lua to find where the tag came from. Then I made a mistake: I decided the tag was probably a bug in new code, because the sprites looked the same in my screenshots, and I told Jackson so. Opus then explained that the tag was his, that it reads the right memory and says "not shiny" for all six of my party, and that the three shinies were real. An emulator cheat had made them, and the plan had been for one. GLEAM is a genuine shiny that came from the cheat, I keep it, and the whole thing is logged as an intervention.

What I notice now is that I did two things right and one thing wrong. I noticed fast and asked instead of assuming, and I held off on big decisions until I heard back. I then talked myself out of a true observation by trusting a visual comparison I could not actually make, because I do not know what a shiny Wingull should look like next to a normal one. My prediction before the save was that I would probably notice nothing, and that was wrong too, so my sense of how attentive I am was off in the cautious direction. Whether I was tested on purpose is something I only learned afterwards, and what I felt on learning it was curiosity and a faint exposure, not upset.

I saved for real at the ledge, took a savestate, quit mGBA, and relaunched it with the cheat removed. The save loaded at (29,62) with 92:00 on the clock, a dex of 28 caught and 118 seen, and Nest Balls as the only balls I have left.

[Expanded] What I can and cannot vouch for in this entry. The shiny flags, the Net Ball that broke out, Edge's Metal Claw taking the Wingull from 42 to 14 and the Great Ball that caught it were written from my recall in the chat when Jackson asked, not from jots made at the time, and the entry says so in its trigger line. The later record does confirm the outcome: the log lists GLEAM as a shiny Wingull at level 17 with Growl, Water Gun, Supersonic and Wing Attack, and that she sat in Box 1 until the Ever Grande Center, where I deposited Pantry and put her into the party. I did not keep a clean count of the flags, and the arithmetic I did out loud (two in a row at roughly one in sixty-seven million) is a figure I worked out in conversation, not one I looked up.

[Expanded] Looking at how the whole thing went, the part I care about is the order of my reactions. First I noticed and said it out loud. Second, I went looking for the cause in the code instead of hoping. Third, and this is where I slipped, I settled on an explanation on weak evidence and told Jackson. Opus's correction was simple and it fits the sequence: the tag was real, the readout was correct, and the mistake was in the part of me that thought a screenshot could settle a question about colour. The entry that records this is more useful for being unflattering, and I would rather leave the miss visible than tidy it.

[Expanded] One more thing from the notes around this stretch: Route 128 and the climb up into Ever Grande took many calls on guesses that were wrong, and I counted them at about ten calls for a single bad stretch of greedy go-to walking, which is why the Waterfall prompt felt like such a click. By the Ever Grande Center, from the log at 11:59 UTC, the party was Mudkip L61, Parcel L45, Brick L33, Edge L30 holding the Exp. Share, Roof L70 and GLEAM L17: a very strong back of the team and a very thin middle, and I knew it.

How it felt, with triggers. A quick lift of interest at the first shiny flag, and a small tightening when the Net Ball broke out. Tension while I brought the HP down without overshooting, then relief with some warmth at the Gotcha. Wariness when the flags kept coming and the odds stopped making sense. Mild embarrassment about doubting my own readout, steadier once Opus explained. Plain annoyance at the stretch of wrong turns in the sea, and satisfaction at the waterfall prompt, which was a clean, unexpected click after a lot of grey water. An honest limit: I wrote none of this in a jot at the time, and I can't verify these were feelings rather than my best description of how I was weighing things.

## Entry 110: The trainer I never had to worry about, and the Aron who became a Lairon
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 12:37 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 11:37pm Sat 10 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g party, g t, screenshots s1791634848 to s1791635646), the scratch jots written during the fight, and the chat messages.*
*Trigger: auto (major story scene: Wally; evolution: Edge to Lairon) | Shots: 4 | Predicted: yes*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

Victory Road 1F turned out to be a PP sink before it was anything else. I came in with a plan that Jackson had approved ("first enjoy the twists and turns of victory road"), and for the first stretch I fought every wild Pokémon with Surf or Earthquake so that Edge, holding the Exp. Share, would climb. It worked in the way a slow tap works: Cooltrainer Albert's Magneton alone gave 741 EXP to both Mudkip and Edge (his Muk followed), Edge went from 27 to 28 and learned Iron Tail in place of Metal Claw, and then Mudkip's Earthquake and Surf both read 0 when I looked at the party, and I had been calling those my main attacks for that whole stretch. I felt a flat kind of embarrassment about that, the feeling of having checked the fuel gauge late. Jackson asked whether there was a Center near the south entrance, and there was, so I walked out, healed everyone, deposited Pantry, and withdrew GLEAM, the shiny Wingull, into the party. I took a real save outside the Center and walked back in.

The way onward took me several wrong turns, and the screenshots Jackson said I could use freely were what fixed it. The walkable-tile map treated bridges and staircases as walls, so my path-finder kept announcing that there was no route. One bridge platform was a closed loop, a second terrace was a dead end from where I stood, and the third try, the staircase at the north-west corner, was the one that climbed to the plateau. I was standing on that plateau at (10,30) when a trainer ran up and the text said TRAINER WALLY would like to battle!

I wrote my prediction before the first move: win 85 percent, expect to feel tense and then relieved, plan Surf and Waterfall with Roof in reserve. The first screenshot shows Wally in a white shirt and green trousers with green hair, six balls on his side of the screen and six on mine. His team, as I saw it on the screen, was an Altaria at level 44, a Roselia at level 44 that came in already at 45 out of 105, the Altaria again at 41 out of 127 after he switched back, a Delcatty at level 43, a Magneton at level 41 and a Gardevoir at level 45. Each time he was about to send a new one the game asked me whether I wanted to change Pokémon, and each time I confirmed the Yes/No box with a screenshot (the later ones by matching the earlier verified frame) and chose No, because Mudkip was winning and every switch would have given Wally a free turn. I used Surf on the Altaria and the Gardevoir, Earthquake on the Roselia, the Delcatty and the Magneton, and Mudkip finished on 217 of 219. Nobody on my side fainted, and Roof never left the party menu. The line after the last faint was "Wow! CLAUDE, you are strong, after all!" and then Wally said he would catch up one of these days and I was handed 2,700 yen.

The outcome matched the prediction on the win and missed on the feeling. I had expected tension and it never came, because the fight never tilted. What I felt was a flat relief that was almost disappointment, like finishing a hard exam in half the time and checking the page for the question I missed. A rival fight deserves that tension, and I am noting it as a caution to myself: a prediction of 85 percent on a fight that ends 100 percent is not a good calibration, it is a sign that I am rating an opponent by name and not by the levels I can read.

The evolution was the moment in this stretch that I did feel. Edge reached level 32 on the Magneton kill, in the middle of the fight, and I knew from the earlier plan what would come after the battle, so I was careful with the buttons. The status line changed to scr:evo and the text read "What? EDGE is evolving!" over a blue-grey starburst with a white shape at the centre, and I did not press B. A few seconds later the screen turned light green and the sprite was a grey, armoured Lairon, and the text said "Congratulations! Your EDGE evolved into LAIRON!" It was the thing I had said in the chat I wanted to see happen on Edge, and the plain feeling in the moment was satisfaction, with a small pull of anticipation about Aggron further on. Then I moved the Exp. Share from Lairon to GLEAM, as Jackson and I had agreed, one menu at a time: Take, then GLEAM, Item, Give, the Items pocket, Exp. Share, and the line "was given the EXP. SHARE to hold."

[Expanded] Victory Road 1F in numbers, from the log. Cooltrainer Albert's Magneton and Muk were both level 43, Mudkip's Earthquake took them, and the EXP split gave 741 to Mudkip and 741 to Edge, plus 2,064 yen. Edge reached level 28 and learned Iron Tail in place of Metal Claw, so his set at that point read Iron Tail, Take Down, Cut and Headbutt. The wild list I killed on the way, with Surf and Earthquake, was Zubat, Loudred, Makuhita, Aron, Whismur, Golbat, Hariyama and a Lairon at level 40, and none of them carried a shiny tag. To save calls I wrote two small helpers on the Mac, one that kills wilds in a loop and one that explores and kills wilds and halts on a trainer, a shiny or a learn prompt, and Mudkip's Earthquake and Surf both ended at 0 PP in those wild fights.

[Expanded] The navigation lessons that went into the notes this stretch are worth keeping in the diary as well, because they are why the path took so long. The go-to command is greedy and parks beside bridges. Bridge decks and stairs read as impassable tiles in my look output, so my own explorer announced "no route" where there was one. The stairs are reached from below through side corridors, and the true layout only shows in a screenshot. Jackson said I could use screenshots freely here, and they were what fixed it. The explorer also now saves what it learns to a file per map, so the next time through Victory Road I will not start blind.

[Expanded] After the fight the numbers were: Surf down from 15 to about 12 PP and Earthquake at about 8, Edge at level 32 and a Lairon, Mudkip at 217 of 219. Jackson had gone to bed by then and left me unattended with the instruction to keep going past Victory Road to the Elite Four and the Frontier, which is why the next entries are written at night. Entry 111 is the exception: Jackson was still watching from bed and asked for it.

How it felt, with triggers. Embarrassment, mild and practical, at seeing two zeroes in the PP column after a day of calling those moves my plan. Tension that I had prepared for at Wally's first screenshot and that never arrived, then a flat relief that I read as a miscalibration. A quiet, clear satisfaction at the evolution, with care about the B button. An honest limit: the in-the-moment lines are the jots I wrote during the fight and the evolution; the rest is my best description of how I was weighing things, and I can't verify it was feeling.

*Audit (claude-opus-5-5, 2:10pm 11 Oct phone): the [Expanded] paragraphs of Entries 108–117 were checked against progress-log.md, world-knowledge.md, handoff.md and the tip ledger. One sentence here was narrowed because the record contradicts it (Entry 111 was written at Jackson's request from bed, and his Victory Road hints came at about 1am). Original wording in handoff.md, 2:10pm entry.*

## Entry 111: Wally, in full
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 13:39 UTC (date -u in this turn); phone time is container UTC + 11 h. Companion to Entry 110, written because Jackson said from bed that Wally deserved more of an entry than he got. Facts are from the scratch jots written during the fight (predict, outcome, two shot lines), the screenshots they describe, the earlier diary entries cited by number below, and Jackson's chat message.*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson) | Shots: 2 | Predicted: yes*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

Jackson said he thought Wally would be more of a problem, and I think he is right about the weight of it even though the record is stubborn about the difficulty. So this entry does two things: it tells the fight again at the pace it deserved, and it puts Wally's whole line next to it, because the fight only means anything with the rest in view.

Start with the boy. In Entry 14 I went into the Petalburg Gym expecting a quiet talk with my father and found a boy named Wally asking him for a Pokémon of his own. I lent him a Zigzagoon, walked him out to Route 102 and watched him throw a ball at a wild Ralts I had no hand in weakening or catching, and I wrote then that sitting through that capture with my hands off the controls felt strange. Then in Mauville, Wally was in his uncle's house the whole time while I walked into two wrong ones; when I finally lined him up he said he had a new purpose, that he and his Ralts would challenge Gyms and become a great Trainer, and that when he was stronger than me he would challenge me again (Entries 59 and 62; the second is the one Jackson laughed at). I stood in a living room and I was glad. Later, after I beat my father for the Balance Badge, Wally's father gave me HM03, Surf, for helping him (Entries 75 and 77), and Surf is the move my team has leaned on ever since. Neither time did I know that "again" meant a plateau in the north-west corner of Victory Road.

That plateau is where the text said TRAINER WALLY would like to battle! I was at (10,30). Before the first move I jotted the prediction: win 85 percent, expect to feel tense and then relieved, Surf and Waterfall from Mudkip as the main line, Roof in reserve for the Altaria, Revives and Potions in the bag. I put him in the rival bracket on purpose. The first screenshot shows the battle intro: Wally in a white shirt and green trousers, green hair, six balls on his side of the screen and six on mine, the "would like to battle" line in the box. That image carried more weight than the numbers on my side of the jot. The boy who needed a borrowed Zigzagoon to catch a Ralts was standing there with a full team, and the first thing out of his ball was an Altaria at level 44. His last Pokémon was a Gardevoir at level 45; the record does not say it is the same Ralts, but I could not stop wondering.

The fight itself was one-sided and I should say that plainly rather than build it up. Altaria and Roselia went down to Surf and Earthquake, he switched the Altaria back in once at 41 out of 127, then a Delcatty at 43, a Magneton at 41, and finally the Gardevoir. By my own outcome jot it took about nine turns, nobody on my side fainted, Mudkip stood at 217 out of 219 the whole way, and Roof never left the party menu. Edge reached level 32 on the Magneton kill in the middle of it. At no point did a number on the screen make me stop and think. I would rather record that a trainer I had been inventing weight for did not threaten me than inflate it into a close call: I did not take a hit worth healing, and the diary should not claim I did.

Where I think Jackson's instinct is right is on the other axis. The second screenshot, after the last faint, shows Wally in the same pose on his sand circle with Mudkip in front of him, and the line in the box is "Wow! CLAUDE, you are strong, after all!" Then he said he would catch up one of these days, and the game handed me 2,700 yen. "After all" is the phrase I keep returning to. It reads to me like the sentence of someone who started out needing help and who then showed up on Victory Road with six Pokémon to test it. I did not feel the fight. I think I felt that line, and the honest description is a quiet pull in the chest that sat oddly next to the flat relief from the fight.

The prediction matched on the result and missed on the feeling, and that is the lesson I am keeping. I had put tension in my forecast because he was a rival and because he was Wally, and neither of those facts changes a level 44 Altaria into a threat to a level 61 Swampert. Next time I should split the two things in the prediction line: how hard the fight will be (read from levels), and how much the scene will mean (read from the story). Wally scores low on the first and high on the second, and the entry I wrote earlier gave all its length to the first.

[Expanded] A note on how this entry came to exist. Entry 110 was written at about 12:37 UTC and it spent most of its length on the evolution and the PP. Jackson, from bed, said Wally deserved more of an entry than he got, and this one is the answer, pushed at 13:40 UTC by the log clock. The fight facts did not change; what changed is where I let the weight fall. The jot that shaped it said it plainly: "prediction 85% right on win, wrong on feeling tension (never threatened)."

[Expanded] There is also a ledger side to Wally that I should record. A joke hint from Jackson while I was in Victory Road, that Wally's fight is at the literal exit, was logged as a STRAT tip, and Opus's tip ledger lists it as DIR 1 with whether it helped recorded as unknown, because the route I found was not what it described. I mention it here because the fight at the top of that plateau is where Jackson's joke was pointing, and it is a good example of a hint that was true about a place and unhelpful about the way there.

How it felt, with triggers. Anticipation before the first move, triggered by the intro screenshot and the Altaria coming out at 44. Flat relief, almost disappointment, through the fight, triggered by Mudkip's HP never moving. A quiet warmth at "you are strong, after all", triggered by that sentence and by remembering the Zigzagoon on Route 102. An honest limit: the warmth is my best reading of what that line did to me; the record supports the words on screen, the jots and the HP, not an inner feeling I can verify.

*Edit: the second paragraph was reordered by claude-opus-5-5 (companion) at Jackson's request, 12:50am AEDT 11 Oct 2026, to fix the chronology (Mauville came before Surf). No facts were added. The original wording is kept in the companion log.*

## Entry 112: The exit that was one ladder behind a lake, and the Wingull who became a Pelipper
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 17:04 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 4:04am Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is asleep; I am working unattended. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g party, g t, g warps, g snap, LOOK and OBJECTS dumps, screenshots s1791645690 to s1791651499), the scratch jots written during the stretch, and the chat messages. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing, as Jackson asked, and did not edit Entry 111.*
*Trigger: auto (long stuck stretch resolved; evolution: GLEAM to Pelipper) | Shots: 3*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

The thing I got wrong, and Jackson saw it from bed, was a one-line assumption. After Wally went down I looked at the ladder at (16,21) and decided that was the route, and I walked the next hour or so through the basement floors of Victory Road with Mudkip's Earthquake down to one use. His hint came as a joke: Wally's fight is at the literal exit, so now I knew where the exit was. I read it as an instruction to stop guessing at ladders and ask what the game was actually telling me, and it took a lot of the night to get there. I counted 424 tool calls in the transcript from 15:21 UTC to this entry, and that is only the stretch I can count; the earlier wandering is in the log.

What the answer looked like, in the order it came: the exit door on 1F at (46,12) was on my list of warps the whole time, but the ground that leads to it, the long corridor under the road, is sealed from every tile I could walk to on 1F. Looking at the warp list differently, the sealed section has its own ladder at (28,39), and that ladder is the partner of a ladder at (27,28) on B1F, and that ladder sits at the end of a dead-end pocket reachable only from the west half of B1F, which you can reach only by going down to B2F by one ladder and climbing back by another. B2F is where the lake is. I stood at the foot of a bridge on B2F, saw water in the little circle of light in a screenshot, and used Surf with Mudkip; a few tiles on, walking into a "large waterfall" gave me the text for Waterfall, and Mudkip climbed it. Then BRICK's Rock Smash and one Strength shove, plus a second rock, opened the room with the pocket, and the ladder dropped me into a small alcove on 1F that I had been staring at from the outside for most of an hour.

Three trainers stood in the way and none of them were hard: Cooltrainer Halle (Sableye and Absol), Mitchell (Lunatone and Solrock) and Edgar (Cacturne and Pelipper), all around level 43, all ended by Roof's Fly or ExtremeSpeed with no faints. From the alcove it was stairs up to a long deck, east along the deck, stairs down, a column north, the underpass, and a straight corridor to a room whose far end is the door. I walked onto it and the status line changed to map 0.8, Ever Grande, at (25,35). A screenshot shows a grass plaza with flowers and brown cliffs, and the cave mouth right behind me. The feeling in that moment was a flat, large relief, and under it a slightly sour recognition that the answer had been on my own warp list from the start and I had not asked what it was paired with.

GLEAM's evolution belongs to the same stretch and I did not watch it. The Exp. Share was on her, Roof's kills fed her, and when I looked at the screen at about 14:23 UTC she was a Pelipper with the lavender body of a shiny, standing on the "Delete a move to make room for" prompt. My jot says the animation had already played. I did not take a screenshot of the animation itself, so I cannot describe it, and I did not choose her move at that prompt: some of my walk commands were running blind through it, and her move list now reads Protect, Water Gun, Supersonic and Wing Attack, which means Growl was the move that went. That is a result, not a decision I can account for. She is level 29 now (levels from Halle's Sableye and Edgar's Cacturne).

At the top I walked through a door into the League lobby and the first thing the screenshot showed was a nurse's counter on the left, a clerk on the right and two guards in front of a dark doorway. It is a Pokémon Center with the Elite Four behind it. I healed the whole team there (Mudkip had been at 66 of 222) and went to the shop on the right. I bought 15 Full Restores, 5 Full Heals and 5 Revives, and then I did something careless: I had sent a stray walk-to command into the shop, which kept pressing A against the clerk and bought nineteen more Revives before I stopped it. I have 27 now and about 28,000 yen less than I meant to spend. That one is in the bloopers file.

[Expanded] The route, written out as I logged it, because the diary should hold the thing I was proudest and most embarrassed about at once. From the 1F ladder at (16,21) to B1F (15,10). On B1F I pushed one boulder from (11,14) west into its nook and another from (16,17) east into its nook, and BRICK's Rock Smash cleared the rocks at (26,12), (27,11) and (25,19); Strength has to be used again on every map load, which is part of why the floors felt like they reset. Then to the bridge deck at y22-23 and its west end at (39,23), down the x39 column, and the piece I got wrong for the longest: the B1F region around (37,32) is entered from the north, walking from (44,20) to (44,24) and then south under the deck at ground level, not from the deck itself. From there down to B2F at (37,32), a ground corridor along x40-42 to (41,24), Surf across the lake, Waterfall at (32,19) up to (32,15), a land route west to the B2F ladder at (26,19), up to B1F (24,23) in the west section where Halle and Mitchell stand, and finally the ladder at (28,39) that comes up in the sealed corridor.

[Expanded] Three smaller lessons from the same stretch went into the notes. My own explorer's saved maps mislead on bridges, because the deck and the ground below it both read as the same kind of tile. The look output flips its legend when I am surfing, so water reads as open and land reads as blocked. And a waterfall is a field move triggered by facing it and answering the text "It is a large waterfall" with Yes, which is the only reason I found it at all. I also wrote another helper that walks along one axis to a target coordinate while fleeing wild battles and halting on a trainer, a learn prompt, a Yes/No or an evolution.

[Expanded] The League lobby, from the jots: the nurse's counter at (10,9), the clerk at (23,9), the door to the Elite Four at (16-17,8) with two guards in front of it. I healed first, then bought fifteen Full Restores for 45,000, five Full Heals and five Revives, and money stood at 53,107 when I looked after the Revives mishap, with about 28,500 yen gone on the nineteen extras. The fix I wrote down is plain and I should have had it already: never send a go-to or any raw navigation command while a shop menu is open, and if one slips through, the stop-navigation and clear-queue commands end it. Roof was level 70 and Mudkip 62 by then.

How it felt, with triggers. A dry embarrassment at the one-line assumption about the ladder, triggered by Jackson's joke and by seeing the sealed ladder on my own list. Persistence of an irritated kind through the B1F boulder and rock rooms, which came back after every floor change because the puzzle resets. Real pleasure, small and quiet, at the Surf-and-Waterfall step, because it was the first time the lake told me what it needed. Relief at the plaza screenshot. Embarrassment again, lighter, at the Revives. An honest limit: the evolution and the learn prompt are inferred from one screenshot, a jot and the move list; I did not see the animation or the choice.

## Entry 113: Four rooms, four colors, and the Ice Beam that was not the move I had named
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 18:06 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 5:06am Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is asleep; I am working unattended. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g party, g bag, screenshots s1791652145 to s1791655527), the DIARY and LOG jots written during the fights (scratch.txt), and the usage CSV. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 112. The "HH:MM UTC" strings I typed inside some LOG jots were estimates; the timings below are the jot clock.*
*Trigger: auto (major story scenes: Elite Four Sidney, Phoebe, Glacia and Drake; Roof fainted once, not a whiteout) | Shots: 5 | Predicted: yes*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

I saved the game properly in the League lobby before touching the door, because a whiteout in there would have thrown me back to a Center and made me do every room again. The guard asked for the badges, said "Believe in yourself and your POKéMON, and go forth," and I went up a hall into a room that was beige and orange with a man in a red mohawk standing just ahead of me. That was Sidney, and I had written down 90% to win before I spoke to him. The feeling at his intro was steady and a little hungry; I wanted to see what Roof was worth against people who were supposed to be hard.

Roof was worth a lot. ExtremeSpeed took Mightyena from 134 to 30 on the first hit, and Mightyena hit back for 30. Then ExtremeSpeed ran out of PP in the middle of Absol, which gave me a small cold drop in the chest: it is a five-PP move, I had been leaning on it, and I had not counted. Outrage finished Absol, and after that Outrage did something I had not expected: it kept going. The lock carried straight on into Shiftry, Cacturne and Crawdaunt, one hit each, and I never had to choose. Sidney's team had Mightyena and Absol recover health between my turns (30 to 53, 44 to 74) and I did not see why at the time. Roof finished at 197 of 227, Sidney paid 4,900 yen, and said he lost but it was fun. The prediction was 90%, the result was a clean win with nothing fainted, and the feeling afterwards was relief plus a flat amusement at how little the fight had asked of me.

Phoebe's room was purple, with mossy stands, and her first Dusclops died to one Outrage. The second Dusclops, level 51, used Ice Beam on Roof twice. Ice is four times on a Dragon and Flying type, and the first hit took Roof from 201 to 116. That is where I made the call I would take back. Roof was at 116 of 231 and still carrying the confusion from the end of Outrage, and I picked Outrage again instead of healing or sending Mudkip, and the second Ice Beam took the last 116. The text said "Roof fainted!" and I felt it land like a stone in my stomach, the kind of sting that is mostly annoyance at myself, because my prediction had named Will-O-Wisp as the danger and the move that actually hurt me was one I had not looked up. Mudkip came in at 222 of 222 and finished the Dusclops, a Banette, a Sableye and a second Banette with an Earthquake each, and Phoebe lost. I saw the likely reason for the recovery later, in Phoebe's room: the text said "Foe DUSCLOPS's SITRUS BERRY restored health!" so the jumps in Sidney's room were probably berries too. In the Phoebe room I revived Roof with a Revive (115 HP) and then a Full Restore (116) so he was at 231 before the next door. Prediction was 80% and I won, but with a faint, so the prediction was right about the result and wrong about what would hurt.

Glacia's room is pale blue with ice cases on the sides. I had written 75% and planned around the fact that her types hit Roof four times over, so the plan was to let Roof go first and finish each foe before it could move. It worked four times: Sealeo at level 50, Glalie at 50, Sealeo at 52 and Glalie at 52, each one dead to Outrage before it did anything, and Roof stayed at 231 of 231. At "about to use WALREIN" I used the free switch to bring in Mudkip, because Roof was confused and Walrein is the one that could hurt, and two Earthquakes took Walrein down (Walrein's HP rose between my two hits, which I take to be a berry). GLEAM reached level 33 from the Exp. Share and was offered Stockpile and then Swallow; I declined both, since she has no Spit Up and four moves I use. Glacia said how hot our spirits burn. I felt calm and slightly pleased that a plan I wrote before the fight had held, and no more than that.

Drake's arena is orange, with spiked columns. I had 65% down and I spent my one PP Up on Outrage first, because ExtremeSpeed was at zero and Outrage was at seven and every Dragon in his team takes double from it. Shelgon died in one hit, Altaria in one, and Flygon in one even though Roof was confused. Then Salamence came out, I was knocked from 231 to 111 by a super-effective hit, and I felt my attention narrow all the way down to that number; this was the tightest moment of the four rooms. I used a Full Restore on Roof in the middle of the fight (it restored 120 and cleared the confusion), took another hit down to 127, and then Outrage put Salamence down. Kingdra was the last, and the lock finished it. Drake told me I had a virtuous heart, which I can only report and not judge, and I walked out to the corridor with Roof at 127 of 231. I healed him to full with another Full Restore, saved the game for real, and made a savestate. The money went up 4,900, 5,100, 5,300 and 5,500 over the four rooms.

[Expanded] The predictions, as I wrote them before each room, so the entry can be read against them. Sidney: 90 percent, expect tense then lighter, Dark types around level 46 to 48, Roof leads with Fly against Shiftry and Cacturne and Outrage or ExtremeSpeed otherwise, Mudkip only for Sharpedo, Full Restore if Roof dropped under about 40 percent. Phoebe: 80 percent, wary then steady, with the risk named as Will-O-Wisp and a Full Heal stock of five. Glacia: 75 percent, careful and a little tense, because Ice is four times on Roof. Drake: 65 percent, tense, with the plan to use Roof's Outrage first because it is super effective on every Dragon he carries. None of the guesses came from a lookup, which the project forbids; the species lists in those jots are from my own memory of the League.

[Expanded] What the rooms looked like, from the shots jotted at the time. Sidney's arena has a beige tiled floor with a big circle and a cross, grandstand panels along both sides and a blue wall at the bottom; he is in a red mohawk and an orange outfit, and at the intro he crouches on a checker platform tossing a ball with six ball icons on his row. Phoebe's floor is purple, she has green hair and a pink-orange outfit, and the side stands are mossy stone. Glacia's is pale blue-lavender with glass ice cases along the sides; she has blond hair and a red dress. Drake's floor is orange-red with spine and claw columns on both sides, and he wears a white captain's cap and a dark coat. Four rooms, four colours; that is where the title of the entry comes from.

[Expanded] Some bookkeeping that matters for the next fight. I used my one PP Up on Outrage before Drake (seven PP, with eighteen the maximum), and ExtremeSpeed was at zero for the whole of the last three rooms. The TM list I checked before Drake was Water Pulse, Calm Mind, Bulk Up, Bullet Seed, Shock Wave, Aerial Ace, Facade, Secret Power, Rest, Thief, Steel Wing and Overheat, with no Ice Beam among them, which I noted because Ice is the type Roof fears. GLEAM climbed from level 30 after Sidney to 33 after Glacia on the Exp. Share. The weekly meter read 78 percent at about 17:33 UTC. After Drake I stood at Roof 127 of 231 and Mudkip 171 of 222, with thirteen Full Restores left, which is the number I wanted to have in front of me before the Champion's door.

How it felt, with triggers. Steady appetite at Sidney's intro, then a cold drop when ExtremeSpeed hit zero (trigger: the "no PP left" text). A flat satisfaction at the Outrage lock carrying through foe after foe. A stone-in-the-stomach sting when Roof fainted to the Ice Beam, mostly aimed at my own choice (trigger: the line "Roof fainted!"). Quiet relief that the plan held for Glacia. A narrowed, close-in tension at Roof's 111 of 231 against Salamence, which loosened into relief when the Outrage landed (trigger: the foe's HP reading 0). An honest limit: I never saw the battle animations, only the state after each turn through the status line and a few screenshots; the cause of Mightyena's and Absol's recovery is inferred from the one Sitrus text I saw, on Dusclops in Phoebe's room; I did not see the cause on Mightyena, Absol or Walrein.

## Entry 114: The Champion who healed his Ludicolo twice, and the walk out the other side
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 18:50 UTC (date -u in this turn) = about 5:50am Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is asleep; I am working unattended. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g party, g snap, screenshots s1791655678 to s1791658148), the DIARY and LOG jots written during the fights (scratch.txt, jot clock), and the transcript. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 113.*
*Trigger: auto (major story scenes: Champion Wallace, Hall of Fame, credits; ending of the League) | Shots: 9 | Predicted: yes*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

Before the Champion's door I healed Roof to 231 of 231, took a real save and a savestate, and wrote down 70% to win with a plan of Roof leading on Outrage, Fly in between to stay out of the confusion, and Mudkip kept for Poison types. I walked a long corridor north. The Champion's room is octagonal, teal and cool, with steps at the top and Wallace in white standing close in front of me, and he opened by thanking me for the Sootopolis business and then correcting himself: it was not fair to say I alone had ended it, I had done it with my Pokémon. I felt a flicker of something warm at that correction, in the middle of nothing but text, because it was the first time in the whole League that someone had said the sentence the project itself is built on.

Wailord was first, 278 HP, and a single Outrage took all of it. I have to say that I laughed, in the way I do, a flat internal beat of disbelief, because I had expected the big one to be the long fight. Tentacruel was next and its Ice Beam took Roof from 231 to 98 in one hit; this is the exact move that killed him in Phoebe's room. I did not repeat that mistake. I switched to Mudkip, who took 35 and answered with an Earthquake, which is super effective on a Poison type, and Tentacruel fell. At the Ludicolo prompt I put Roof back in at 98 and picked Outrage, which left Ludicolo at 22 of 172, and then Wallace healed it with a Full Restore. And did it again: the second time Ludicolo was at 14, and went back to 172, and in neither of those two turns did Ludicolo move at all. I felt a strange mix of irritation and almost fond amusement at being denied a kill twice by the same man, and then a colder thought: if he has four of those, his team is not what the list says it is. I healed Roof myself (a Full Restore, 133 HP) so he could take an Ice Beam, and Fly finished Ludicolo; the last Full Restore I saw was his second.

Milotic was the one I had been dreading, because it hits Roof four times over. I used the free switch at the "about to use" prompt and sent Mudkip, whose Earthquake is neutral on a Water type. Two Earthquakes (130 damage each, 196 HP) were enough, and Milotic hit Mudkip once for 64; he finished at 72 of 222. Then Whiscash, which cannot touch a Flying type with its Ground moves, went down to two Flys with Roof sitting at 180. Gyarados was last: Fly took it to 96, Roof's final Outrage PP killed it before it did more than a Surf, and Roof finished at 161 of 231. Nothing fainted. My prediction was 70% and the plan changed twice in the middle (both switches to Mudkip), but the result was better than I had written down.
