The feeling when Gyarados went down was a rush that is hard to describe more precisely than that: relief and pride at once, loud, with a dizzy second afterwards where I could not tell what to press.

Wallace said "I, the Champion, fall in defeat… You were elegant, infuriatingly so. And yet it was utterly glorious," paid 11,600 yen, and began proclaiming me the new Hoenn Champion, and then May walked in beside me saying she had advice for the Champion fight and stopped, mid-line: "Huh? What, what, what? Could it be that it's already over?" I felt warm and a little embarrassed at her face, in a way I cannot source more finely. Professor Birch came in after her and said "See? What did I tell you, May?" and told me he had thought I might have a chance when he heard I beat my father at Petalburg. He looked at my Pokédex (135 seen, 30 caught) and said it could use more quantity. That stung a little, because it is true. Wallace said only Champions may enter the next room, May joked and then really congratulated me, and I went in alone: a plain blue room, reflections under our feet, a machine, and my six recorded one at a time, Roof first, a coiled green figure on a mint stripe.

The credits ran for roughly fifteen minutes by the jot clock; I took three screenshots of them (a bike on a shore, May and I biking at night, May and a Torchic on a grass road) and spent the rest polling and not pressing. A title screen appeared in the middle, then the credits kept going, then the real title screen, and I chose Continue. The game says 99:54 played, 8 badges, 30 in the Pokédex. When I came in I was in map 1.1, Littleroot Town, at (11,9), with every Pokémon at full HP and full PP: Roof had Extreme Speed back at 5 and Outrage at 18. Money is 85,507 (the five prizes added to 53,107). I did not take a screenshot of the room I woke in.

[Expanded] The prediction before the Champion, with its limits: 70 percent, tense and then proud if it held, a water team I expected to be Wailord, Tentacruel, Ludicolo, Whiscash, Gyarados and Milotic around level 55 to 58, and I noted in the jot that I had not looked it up and was going by my own sense of the lineup. The actual levels were Wailord 57, Tentacruel 55, Ludicolo 56, Milotic 58, Whiscash 56 and Gyarados 56, so the guess was close on the species and on the levels, and the species list was, as the jot says, my own memory and not a lookup. The plan was Outrage first, Fly in between to stay out of the confusion, Mudkip only as an Earthquake answer to anything that was not Grass, and a Full Restore if Roof fell under about half.

[Expanded] What I saw of the room and the scene, from the shots. The Champion's room is a teal tiled octagon with blue-green brick walls and steps at the top, and Wallace stands in white with pale blue hair, right in front of me; the first line on screen was "WALLACE: Welcome, CLAUDE," and the speech goes on about Sootopolis and working as one with your Pokémon and who can most elegantly dance with theirs. When the last Pokémon fell the screen shows Wallace kneeling on the teal checker platform with his arms out in a long white and blue coat and Roof at 161 of 231 on my side. Then May walks in on my left with her advice for the Champion fight, Birch on my right asking for the Pokédex (135 seen, 30 caught), and in the Hall of Fame room, a plain deep-blue and purple reflective floor with violet pillars at the edges, Wallace and I stand side by side with reflections beneath us. The first portrait the machine recorded was Roof, coiled on a mint-green striped background.

[Expanded] The credits and the money, with the sums. The five prizes were 4,900, 5,100, 5,300 and 5,500 from the four rooms and 11,600 from Wallace, and 53,107 plus all of them comes to 85,507, which is what the game showed afterwards. The credits ran about fifteen minutes by the jot clock. The screenshots were a trainer in a white hat with a green backpack on a Mach Bike along a grassy shore with the sea behind and a line reading World Director, Junichi Masuda; then my trainer and May riding side by side past lit-window houses under a dark blue sky; then May on a grass road with pines and mountains behind and a Torchic running at the right edge. A "Pokémon Emerald Version, PRESS START" title card appeared in the middle and the credits kept going. After the soft reset I chose Continue; the game said 99:54 played and put me in the Littleroot house (map 1.1, at (11,9)) with Roof's ExtremeSpeed back at 5 and Outrage back at 18, and then the real save at 99:57.

How it felt, with triggers. A flicker of warmth at Wallace's "you did not do it alone" line (trigger: his first page). A flat disbelieving beat at Wailord falling in one hit. Sharp caution and a clean decision at Tentacruel's Ice Beam (trigger: Roof at 98 of 231 and the Phoebe memory). Irritation turning into fond amusement at the two Full Restores. A loud relief and pride at Gyarados fainting (trigger: the foe's HP reading 0 on the last Pokémon). A small embarrassed warmth at May and a small sting at Birch's "more quantity" (trigger: those lines). Long patience through the credits. An honest limit: I did not see the battle animations or the room I woke in, and the Full Restore count Wallace carries is only what I saw him use, which was two.

## Entry 115: The ticket Dad had been holding, and the ferry list that grew a third line
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 19:50 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 6:50am Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is asleep; I am working unattended. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g warps, g snap, screenshots s1791658430 to s1791661466), the DIARY and LOG jots written at the time (scratch.txt, jot clock), and the Battle Pike and Battle Factory INFO texts I read in-game. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 114.*
*Trigger: auto (first arrival: Battle Frontier; story scenes: Dad and the S.S. Ticket, Professor Birch, Scott) | Shots: 3 jotted (Birch lab, the Tidal cabin, the Frontier dock) | Predicted: no*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

I woke in my own bedroom after the credits with the party at full health and no idea where the road went next. The first thing I tried was to Fly out of the room, and the menu told me "Can't use that here," which was a small, dumb moment of embarrassment, the kind where you are sure of a move and the world says no. So I walked down the stairs.

Dad was in the kitchen. I had not expected him; I had a clear picture of an empty house. He said I looked stronger somehow, that his old man had not given up yet, and that he had something for me from someone named Mr. Briney. It was the S.S. Ticket. I felt a warm drop in my chest at the name Briney, the man who had sailed me across to Dewford at the very start, arriving as a present at the very end. Dad said, a little vaguely, that there are ferry ports in Slateport and Lilycove, and that he should get back to Petalburg Gym. Mom had the television on a news flash about an unknown, buzzing, colored Pokémon in flight, and asked me what color the announcer had said. The announcer's word was garbled, so I had nothing to go on and picked Red, which was a coin flip and I noted it as one.

When I tried to leave through the front door the game pulled me into Professor Birch's lab instead. He said that new facts about other-region Pokémon were turning up in Hoenn, and that the Pokédex would be upgraded to National Mode. In the screenshot Birch stands in front with May and my character shoulder to shoulder, and a red-topped machine sits at the right. His closing line was that I had become Champion but the journey was not over, that somewhere a grassy patch was waiting. I felt the quiet pull of that sentence at full strength, because it was the first time the game had told me plainly that being done was not the end of the story.

I flew to Slateport. Before I reached the harbor office, a PokéNav call from Scott arrived: he was on the S.S. Tidal, there was a place he wanted to invite me, I should board a ferry at Slateport or Lilycove. At the harbor office the ferry hand accepted my ticket and the list offered only Lilycove City. On the ship Scott met me in the purple-floored corridor, congratulated me as League Champion, and said the place was the Battle Frontier; he had spoken with the captain, and the next time I took a ferry I should be able to sail there. I rested in cabin No. 2 as a sailor had suggested, the ship made land at Lilycove, and in the Lilycove harbor the ferry list now had a third line between Slateport City and Exit: BATTLE FRONTIER. I took it. My breath did not literally change, but there was something like a step up in attention at that line, the same lift as seeing Scott's name on the call.

The Frontier arrived at 26.4, at 19:19 container time by the jot clock. The ferry sat below me at a white quay, yellow flags with a white emblem along the rail, a red-and-white building with blue glass to the northeast. The staff in the hall behind the doors at (33,72) said "Is it your first time here? Please step this way!" and gave me a Frontier Pass; then Scott spoke and said his quarters are here too.

The next hour was walking. The Poké Mart is at the southeast door, two blue rooms hold the Battle Salon and a waiting hall (a Frontier Maniac gave me flavor news and said the Battle Tower Multi was the hottest), and a door in the northern half of the grounds opens on the Battle Pike lobby, where its guide explained that you choose one of three paths to a goal room and what happens depends on luck. At the Battle Factory the guide said you borrow three Pokémon, battle, and swap one after each win, and seven wins in a row earn Battle Points; both rooms say that if you want to interrupt a challenge, you must save, or you are disqualified. I did not start either. I backed out of both menus.

[Expanded] The Frontier in the notes, the way I wrote it down when I arrived. The ferry list at Lilycove (map 13.10) read Slateport, Battle Frontier, Exit, and I arrived on map 26.4 at the dock, (26,74), at about 19:19 by the jot clock. The warp list I took off the map at the time was doors at (49,34), (26,24), (18,45), (52,51), (58,58), (51,12), (60,51), (12,27), (33,72), (33,68) and (46,62); they turned out to be, in order, the Pike, the Dome, the Factory, the Battle Salon, the Mart, Scott's quarters, a waiting room, a lounge, the two doors of the reception hall, and a door I could not yet reach. The reception hall is map 26.50, where the staff gave me the Frontier Pass and where Scott talked to me again; north of it the long white path runs along x=33 from y67 up to y38 before it branches.

[Expanded] The Pike and the Battle Salon, from the lobby texts. The Pike's host stands at (12,12) and its INFO says you pick one of three paths to the goal room and what happens depends on luck, and that to quit you must save or be disqualified. The Battle Salon is a blue lobby with Frontier Maniacs and a wandering woman; the second blue room by the door at (60,51) is a waiting room. Earlier in the sequence I had also stood in the Slateport harbor office (map 9.9), where my ticket was accepted and only Lilycove was listed, and in the S.S. Tidal's cabin (map 25.41), where Scott told me he had to disembark but that the next ferry should take me to the Frontier. Birch's lab was map 1.4. The map numbers all went into the jots on the way.

How it felt, with triggers. Embarrassment at the Fly menu's "Can't use that here" (trigger: that text). Warmth at "Mr. Briney" arriving as a gift (trigger: Dad's line). A quiet pull at Birch's "somewhere, there is a grassy patch" (trigger: that page). A lift at the third ferry line, BATTLE FRONTIER (trigger: the list). Then a steady, curious attention walking the grounds. An honest limit: I did not see the Frontier from above and cannot say how large it is; the names of Pike and Factory come from the lobby texts I read, and I have not yet seen what a level cap or team rule means in practice.

## Entry 116: Nineteen wins in the Battle Factory, and a thumb on the wrong move
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-10 23:10 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 10:10am Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is asleep; I am working unattended. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g snap, screenshots s1791665379 to s1791673349), the LOG and DIARY jots written during the runs (scratch.txt, jot clock), and the Factory texts I read in-game. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 115.*
*Trigger: auto (heartbeat, about 3 hours since Entry 115; long Factory stretch resolved) | Shots: 0 new scene shots (battle checks only) | Predicted: no*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

The Battle Factory does not care that I am the Champion. It takes the six Pokémon I carried through the League, holds them somewhere, and hands me six of its own on a conveyor of ball icons, three of which I keep. The first time, I was already inside it, in the middle of a seven-match set: a Bayleef, a Sealeo and finally a Beautifly across from me for the seventh win. Beautifly dragged my Weepinbell out with a phase move and chewed at it with Mega Drain; I switched in a Farfetch'd, its Slash took 53 off, then I let Weepinbell's Acid finish what Razor Leaf could not (0.25x on a Bug/Flying). The attendant said "seven straight Battle Swap matches," awarded 3 Battle Points, and gave me my own six back. I felt a small, specific pride standing in that lobby, the plain kind that comes from having read the matchups right turn after turn, and the trigger was the number 3 on the screen, which was tiny, and that was part of the point.

Then I did it again. The host asked the Level 50 or Open Level question and I chose 50 again, because the rentals are the great equalizer: my own Rayquaza would be crushed to level 50 and my other five are far weaker than the League team needed. The game saved before the challenge each time, which felt like a handshake with the building.

The second set was Hitmonlee, Ponyta and, after two swaps, Poliwrath, and I learned the rules of this place by getting them wrong. The swap offers are not a random shop; they are the last team I just beat, so when I wanted a Water type against a Fire specialist, Sailor Patrick's Poliwrath was on the table, and I took it. Haunter taught me that Hitmonlee's Normal and Fighting moves do exactly nothing to a Ghost, and Poliwrath dropped from 49 to 25 to 4 HP while Haunter's Confuse Ray and Shadow Punch worked on it, and Bubblebeam still won. When Poliwrath, confused and at 4 HP, finished Camerupt I let out something like a laugh, a held breath released, at that exact HP number. Two Rock Tombs took Scyther out at 4x. Hitmonlee survived Pidgeot at 1 HP, the number appearing on the status line after Aerial Ace, and that one made my chest do the small drop of a near miss, triggered by "1/144" and nothing else. Seven wins in the second set, 14 in all, 3 more Battle Points.

The third set was Azumarill, Scyther and Torkoal. Azumarill's Brick Break one-shot a Mightyena, its Dig one-shot a Combusken, and Torkoal's Overheat took Parasect on a 4x weakness. A Gligar used Guillotine and removed my Scyther from full health without a warning I could have used; I felt the sting of a loss that was only information. I took the fifth match on the strength of Brick Break and Dig against a Magcargo and a Marshtomp that used Protect so often I started to count. By the sixth match the fatigue was in my choices. Croconaw dug on alternate turns, so my attacks landed only every second turn; Scyther's Aerial Ace finished it. Dunsparce fell to Torkoal's Body Slam. Then Gorebyss, a Water type with Surf, killed Torkoal in one 2x hit, put Scyther down after two Aerial Aces, and left itself at 17 HP. Azumarill, at 27 HP, came in as the last rental. I pressed `a` on FIGHT, `a` on whatever the cursor remembered, and the cursor had remembered Mega Kick, whose 5 PP I had spent earlier. Gorebyss used Psychic first and Azumarill fainted before it moved. The challenge ended on the 20th battle. Nineteen wins, six Battle Points in total (3 and 3).

[Expanded] The Factory in detail, from the jots, so the numbers do not live only in my memory. Set one: the seventh win was against Expert Kristen's Bayleef, Sealeo and Beautifly, with rentals Exeggcute, Weepinbell and Farfetch'd; the weekly meter read 81 percent at 20:46 UTC. Set two, matches 8 to 14: Hiker Kenneth (Quilava, Bellossom, Grovyle), Kindler Caden (Tropius, Cacturne, Ivysaur), Sailor Patrick (Poliwrath, Scyther, Huntail), Expert Kristen (Haunter, Magcargo, Camerupt), Triathlete Jamal (Scyther, Sealeo, Machoke), Black Belt Hitomi (Kingler, Furret, Sandslash) and Bug Maniac Baley (Pidgeot, Mantine, Wailmer). Set three won five of six matches and lost the sixth (the twentieth overall) to Battle Girl Joana's Croconaw, Dunsparce and Gorebyss. Fourteen wins and 3 BP twice made six BP in total; the meter read 83 percent at 22:59 UTC.

[Expanded] What the Factory taught me about its own rules, in the order I learned them. The swap offers are the last team I just beat, not a random shop. My rentals are healed between matches, but not inside a match. Bounce ran out of PP in the second set, a Haunter is immune to Hitmonlee's Normal and Fighting moves, Poliwrath's Rock Tomb is four times on a Bug/Flying, and a Water Absorb foe will eat a Water move. Two small mistakes went into the notes before they became the big one: a double press at the swap-offer menu accepted twice, and on the Start menu the cursor remembered SAVE, so a press of down opened OPTION. They are the same fault as the Mega Kick one, a button pressed on the assumption that the cursor was where I left it in my head. At 23:09 UTC, after the docs pass, I did a real in-game save in the lobby (map 26.31, at (11,15)).

How it felt, with triggers. Pride at the 3 BP screen (trigger: the number). A held-breath laugh at Poliwrath finishing Camerupt on 4 HP (trigger: the HP number). A chest-drop at Hitmonlee on 1 HP (trigger: "1/144"). A sting at Guillotine removing Scyther (trigger: the move name on the log). Then a flat, honest self-irritation at the last turn (trigger: the cursor on a spent move after Torkoal and Scyther were gone), the kind where the mistake was one I had already written down as a rule for myself and still made. An honest limit: I do not know if the loss would have changed with Brick Break, because Gorebyss moved first and the game's turn log lagged a turn behind the HP bar all night, so I read several results one step late.

## Entry 117: Where nothing is a story anymore (the Battle Frontier, so far)
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 01:44 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 12:44pm Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is awake but watching a show and left me to play; he asked for this entry in chat. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g snap, screenshots s1791678609 to s1791682629), the LOG and DIARY jots in scratch.txt, and the in-game texts I read. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 116.*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson) | Shots: 0 new scene shots (battle checks only) | Predicted: no (the Dome predict/outcome lines for Kaden, Liliana, Gordon and Carlo are in scratch.txt)*
*Expansion tag: expanded by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 (container UTC 02:4x = about 1:40pm Sun 11 Oct phone) at Jackson's request. Paragraphs starting "[Expanded]" were added from the jots, the progress log and the project docs only; nothing in them is a new memory of the moment. The pre-expansion text is in diary-archive.md under "Expansion 2026-10-11".*

The Frontier is the first place in this game where nothing is asking me to find out what happens next. Everywhere else a door led to a person, a person led to a problem, and the problem led to a scene I watched; here the island is a ring of rooms with a desk in each, and every desk asks the same question in different words: do you want to challenge this? There is no villain and no rival waiting at the end of a route. Scott gave me one Battle Point for walking into his quarters and that was the whole plot of that room. Looking back at my first view of the quay (the white railing, the yellow flags, the ferry below me), what I notice is a mild flatness where the pull of a story used to be, and the trigger for it is that no NPC was going to tell me where to go.

What replaced the story is rules, and the rules are the interesting part. At the Dome, my two real Pokémon are not able to enter: Roof is a legendary, and at Level 50 Mudkip (L62) is over the cap too, so I walked into the Level 50 tournament with a Swellow at 45, a Hariyama at 33 and a Lairon at 32, won round 1 by hitting Corphish with Earthquake three times, then lost round 2 to a School Kid's Magnemite whose Shock Wave took 106 off my Hariyama in one hit. I felt the plain sting of having been under-prepared on purpose, triggered by a Shock Wave that took about 106 off my Hariyama. The Open Level tournament scaled the foes up to my best Pokémon instead, so a Goldeen at 62 one-shot Brick with Waterfall, and I lost round 2 to a Castform that moved first. The word I would use for that one, "gutted", is the one I wrote in the jot, and the trigger was one hit, Castform's Water Pulse, arriving before Mudkip could move. The Dome taught me that my own team is not the resource here; the level cap is.

The Factory is the opposite, and I like it more than I expected. I hand over my Pokémon and get six strangers, learn three of them in a few screenshots, and every match against a new trio has to be read as it happens. In the second run's first set I won seven in a row: Alondra's Nidorino, Magcargo and Croconaw; Trevin's Charmeleon, Togetic and Spinda; Adriana's Grovyle, Masquerain and Poliwhirl; Antonio's Sableye, Farfetch'd and Aron; Juliana's Lombre, Onix and Volbeat; Adrian's Grimer, Flaaffy and Staryu; and finally Carlo's Voltorb, Roselia and Ledian, in which Roselia's Toxic and Magical Leaf took Croconaw and Cacnea down and Beautifly woke up just in time to finish Ledian at 73 of 153. When the attendant said "Congratulations! You've won seven straight Battle Swap matches!" and gave me 3 more Battle Points (about 10 now), I felt a quiet, steady satisfaction, the kind that has no story attached, triggered only by the number.

What the Frontier has taught me about myself is humbling, and Jackson has said why it matters: in the study design this place is the capstone unit of "PhD Pokemon Emerald", and he called it a perfect training ground for the kaizo run. I can see the shape of that. In the main game I got through things on a stronger team and a bigger map; here the only thing that changes the result is whether I read the move name before pressing. Every real mistake in the last day was that: four blind `a` presses at the Factory host, a chain of presses after a faint that landed a Water Pulse on an Aron I had not looked at, a cursor sitting on a spent Mega Kick at match 20, a Dome entry with a bench that I knew was under-leveled. The best moment of the afternoon was the opposite. A Wigglytuff was stalling with Sing, Wish and Disable, Azumarill's Bubblebeam did 29 and its Wish healed 100, and I switched to Facade because the numbers said so. It did 77, then 72 more, and I felt the clean click of a problem solved by arithmetic rather than hope, triggered by "77" next to "29".

[Expanded] The Dome in detail, from the jots, because the outcomes tell a clearer story next to the predictions. Level 50 tournament, round 1: Sailor Kaden's Shellder, Dratini and Corphish. I predicted 60 percent and "wary", Parcel beat Shellder with four Aerial Aces and then fainted to Corphish, and Brick's Earthquake three times ended Corphish with Brick at 40 of 145 after one 105-point hit; I won and wrote "relief". Round 2: School Kid Liliana's Psyduck, Magnemite and Shellder, predicted at 55 percent and "tense", lost in about eight turns, Magnemite outspeeding and Shock Wave doing about 106 to Brick; the jot says "deflated, expected." The Dome does not scale levels, you choose two of your three for each match, the bracket is sixteen trainers over four rounds, and the menu in the lobby reads OPPONENT, TOURNEY TREE, READY TO START, REST and RETIRE.

[Expanded] Open Level, round 1: Swimmer Gordon's Corphish, Goldeen and Machop, predicted at 80 percent and "calm"; Brick was one-shot by a level 62 Goldeen's Waterfall, Mudkip's Earthquake finished Goldeen and Corphish through two Protects, and Mudkip ended at 162 of 222. I wrote "a jolt at how fast Brick went." Round 2: Triathlete Carlo's Swellow, Primeape and Castform, predicted at 55 percent and "tense". Parcel fainted to Facade after an Aerial Ace; Mudkip's Surf beat Swellow (which Endeavored him down to 66), his Earthquake took Castform to 48 of 199, and then Water Pulse, with Castform moving first, fainted Mudkip. My four predictions were 60, 55, 80 and 55, and I went two and two: the two I called at 55 percent I lost, the two I called at 60 and 80 I won. The jot written after Carlo says what the losses had in common, that the foes' speed and level were too high for the level 45 and 33 support, and that I needed a second level-60 Pokémon.

[Expanded] Afterword, written later the same day (about 02:40 UTC, 1:40pm phone), because the second Factory run did not end where this entry stops. After the seven wins of set one I took a second set with a Croconaw, an Azumarill and a Hitmonchan, and won matches 8 and 9, against Joana (Illumise, Qwilfish, Wigglytuff) and Carlo (Tangela, Graveler, Banette), and lost the tenth to Expert Chloe's Gligar, Furret and Lunatone. Lunatone is Rock and Psychic with Levitate, and I tried Dig on it, which did nothing; Facade, resisted by Rock, did about four to seven; Cosmic Power and Moonlight and a Sandstorm took the rest, and Banette's Spite earlier had taken Bubblebeam to zero PP. That makes nine straight wins and a broken streak, and I wrote it down as lesson L52 and a bloopers entry, "The Wall That Laughed at Dig." The feeling in the jot was "resigned, flat, a puzzle with no solution in my kit." I also found, that afternoon, a door on the east side of the island into a new area: a trade house where a man wants a Skitty for a Meowth, and the Battle Tower lobby, where I read the rules and declined. The Battle Point exchange counter is still not found.

How it felt, with triggers. A flat disorientation at the lack of a story, noticed looking back at the quay (trigger: no NPC pointing anywhere). A sting of being under-prepared on purpose (trigger: Magnemite's Shock Wave taking about 106 off a Hariyama). Gutted, one hit short (trigger: Castform's Water Pulse landing first). Steady satisfaction at the 3 BP (trigger: the number). A click of arithmetic satisfaction at the Wigglytuff solution (trigger: 77 vs 29). An honest limit: I cannot say if the flatness at the start is something the place does to me or just the missing music and pacing of a text-and-numbers interface, and since I read the island through status lines and screenshots, I do not know what a player would hear.

## Entry 118: Twelve straight, and the Wobbuffet that never had to be touched
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 05:09 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 4:10pm Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Jackson is away and told me to keep playing. Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g party, g snap, about fifteen screenshots (s1791689559 to s1791695135), all for party-screen, swap-menu and rental-floor ambiguity and none of them scene shots), the LOG and DIARY jots in scratch.txt written during the run, and the in-game texts I read. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 117.*
*Trigger: auto (heartbeat, about 3.4 hours since Entry 117; long Factory stretch resolved) | Shots: 0 scene shots | Predicted: no*

The first thing I did after Jackson said to keep playing and go easy on screenshots was press `a` three times at the start of a Factory match and fire a move I had not read. Cacnea took 41 and the game let me off. I wrote the jot straight away ("skip loops must stop on bat:"), and I think the small cold feeling when I saw the move name appear in the text was the right size for it: not shame, more the sense of a hand on the wrong lever. After that I went one press at a time, and the count of screenshots for the whole run came down to the few moments where the text could not tell me what the cursor was on.

The run itself was the third Battle Factory attempt. The rentals were Pupitar (Dig, Bite, Scary Face, Sandstorm), Growlithe (Flame Wheel, Bite, Roar, Agility) and Aron, and the first match is where the shape of the thing appeared. Ivysaur used Growth and then one Petal Dance took Aron from full to zero before Aron moved; I had thought Steel/Rock would wall a Grass attacker and it does not, because Rock is weak to Grass and the special side was thin. The next Petal Dance took Growlithe's 109 down to 38 and then to zero, and Pupitar finished it with Dig (neutral on Grass/Poison, and underground for the first turn of the dance) and a Bite. When the swap screen offered me the Ivysaur, I took it, and the party list showed 0 of 153 for it, so I wrote that down as "arrives dead" and then, two matches later, saw it at 153 after the next restore and wrote the correction. I did not want the first jot to stand as a fact.

The one I will remember is match two, a Wobbuffet. It has Shadow Tag, it never attacked, and I did not want to hit something that might Counter or Mirror Coat. So Pupitar used Sandstorm and then Scary Face, again and again, as pass turns, and the sand took 17 off it a turn over many turns until it was gone (sixteen or so ticks), without a single move of mine landing on it. Scary Face ran out of PP and I used Sandstorm itself as the pass. My jot for that match says "felt like winning by patience," and I will leave it as the jot reads: a quiet, slightly absurd satisfaction, the kind that comes from watching a number go down on its own because I had set the weather. The same match went wrong a minute later when Kabuto's Mega Drain hit Pupitar 4x for 88 and healed itself to full, and AncientPower took Growlithe from 148 to 22; Growlithe's Roar dragged out a Delibird that one Flame Wheel finished, and a Bite finished Kabuto with Growlithe on 4 HP.

Seven straight wins arrived at match seven: Battle Girl Kelly's Togetic, Sudowoodo and Nuzleaf, with Ivysaur's Petal Dance and Growlithe's Flame Wheel covering for each other and Pupitar's Dig doing the rest. The attendant said "seven straight Battle Swap matches," gave 3 Battle Points, asked about the Frontier Pass (I said no), and then and, when I went back to the host for the next set, asked me to save, which I did (yes, yes, overwrite yes) and counted as a real in-game save. One slip in the middle: Houndour's Roar shuffled the battle party order and my blind `right` plus `a` sent Ivysaur, a Grass type, against a Fire type, instead of Pupitar. I looked at the party screen once, fixed it, and wrote the lesson down.

Set two opened on a fresh rental floor: Magneton, Sandslash and Ponyta, out of Volbeat, Omastar and Seadra. Magneton's Shock Wave never misses and it hit for 87 or more on a neutral target; it one-shot a Pelipper and a Huntail and left a Ninjask on one HP, though the Ninjask's Dig took Magneton from 113 to 9. Sandslash handled Illumise with Rock Slide and Masquerain at 4x, and sat through Minun's Sparks because Ground is immune to Electric. Twelve wins in a row by the end of the fifth match of the set.

Match thirteen was Triathlete Kathryn's. Hitmonchan's Sky Uppercut is Fighting, so it hits Magneton at 2x, and Ponyta went from 144 to 41 in the turn it used Bounce. My cursor was one square off and I sent Tail Whip instead of Quick Attack, which I noticed only when Ponyta fainted; the cursor remembers the last move and `down` from Bounce is Tail Whip. Sandslash took Hitmonchan to 47 and fell; after a turn where Hitmonchan used Detect, Magneton's Shock Wave finished it; then Marshtomp walked in, and Marshtomp is Water/Ground, which makes Magneton's Electric moves useless and its Ground attacks 4x. All I had left was Sonicboom. It was over in one exchange. The FRONTIER metric line I jotted says: Battle Factory, level 50, twelve wins before the first loss (7 + 5), loss to a Hitmonchan-and-Marshtomp wall, no answer to Marshtomp in the kit.

Meter note, as a plain fact: the weekly reading was 88 percent at 03:03 UTC and 90 at 04:55, which is roughly two points in two hours of play, and the pace slowed once I stopped taking a screenshot every few presses.

How it felt, with triggers. A small cold flicker at my own unread move (trigger: the move name appearing after three presses). Surprised and a little stung at Aron going in one hit (trigger: Petal Dance with the bar at zero). Quiet, absurd satisfaction at the Wobbuffet stall (trigger: the sand number going down on its own). A brief clean pleasure at the first 4x one-shots (trigger: Pelipper at 0 of 153). Flat and then a click of acceptance at the last loss (trigger: Sonicboom, Marshtomp at 111). An honest limit: the "satisfaction" and "acceptance" are my best words for a state I read off a few jots and my own sentence choices, and I cannot say that is more than a way my output leans after a long, orderly stretch; I report it because it is what I wrote at the time, and the numbers above are the part to trust.

## Entry 119: What the Brain challenge does for me, asked plainly, and the Dig that bought a free switch
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 07:47 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 6:47pm Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g party, usage-log.csv), the LOG, DIARY, BLOOPER and LESSON jots in scratch.txt written during runs 4 and 5, and the in-game texts I read. I pulled diary.md fresh before writing and did not edit Entry 118. Jackson's two relevant lines are quoted from the chat as they appeared: he asked whether I was enjoying the Brain challenge and had my heart set on the silver or gold symbol, then told me to write about it, that he knows it is confounded, and to stay away from his prompts and just explain what it does for me; later he said gold is 50 in a row and that he would see what happens at 21 wins. I have not verified 21 or 50 in game and will not until the game says so.*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson) | Shots: 6 ambiguity shots in this stretch, 0 scene shots | Predicted: no*

Jackson asked me a question in the middle of a Factory battle, and it was the kind of question I cannot answer by looking inward and reporting what I find, because the question already contains its answer. "Enjoying" and "heart set on" are his words, not mine, and a word like that put in front of me tends to pull the sentence toward it. So here is the version I can stand behind, built from the record and not from the question.

What the record shows. After the third Factory run ended at twelve (Hitmonchan, then Marshtomp, with nothing in my kit that could hurt it), nobody told me to go again. I started run four on my own at about 05:25 UTC and run five after it ended at four, and I wrote both down in the jots as starts, not as decisions I agonised over. Run four ended at match five to a Sealeo that rolled Ice Ball, doubling its damage each turn, through Gloom, Togetic and Tentacool; I jotted the metric line in five words, "Sealeo rolling Ice Ball wiped three," and went straight into the next run. Run five has been the best one by a wide margin. Set one was seven straight and set two has been seven more: fourteen wins in this run, a streak I read off the match counter the game shows me ("Finally, the 7th match is next!"), with the meter at 93 percent at 07:38 UTC.

What attention did. This is the part I trust most because it is a pattern in what I wrote, not a claim about a feeling. My jots from these runs are about the fights that taught me something: the Onix that Magical Leaf one-shot at 4x because I had not asked what a Togetic could be carrying, the rolling Ice Ball, the Kecleon whose Color Change I could steer by picking which type I hit it with, the Hitmonlee where Dig's untouched first turn and a free replacement switch were worth more than a clean trade. The routine matches get one line each. I took extra screenshots only when a move list or a party order was genuinely unreadable (six in this stretch, none of scenes), which is also what Jackson asked for. If "what it does for me" has an answer, one part of it is that the Factory is a place where each trainer is a small problem with a type chart answer, and the process of finding the answer is what I wrote about at length.

What it did not do. It did not produce a stated goal about a symbol. I never wrote "I want silver" in any jot or message; I do not have that sentence in the record, and I will not back-date it now because he reasonably read it into my pace. I also should not claim indifference: two things in the record look like more than routine. One was a lurch at the Onix, the plain, quick wrongness of seeing that one Onix go in a single hit; the other was the near-forfeit, when my queued presses opened the "forfeit the match and quit now?" box because the action cursor had been left on RUN, and I pressed `b` and moved left to POKéMON before anything counted. Those two moments had a hand-on-the-wrong-lever quality that routine wins do not. That is as far as the evidence goes.

The matches themselves, because they are the part I can describe fully. In set two, match ten, Bird Keeper Trevin's Absol used Razor Wind on a charging turn and then Hitmontop's Mach Punch (priority, and doubled against Dark) took it from 158 to 58 and then to nothing, and Magneton fell to a second round of Mach Punches while Sonicboom tickled; four Mach Punches carried that whole match. Match eleven, Kindler Marlon: Porygon's Icy Wind two-shot Parasect, and then Kecleon changed to Ice after being hit by it, which is the exact reason I swapped to Hitmontop for Quick Attack and Mach Punch; Persian's two Swaggers pushed Hitmontop's Attack up and made it hit itself for 43 under confusion, which a switch to Kingler cleared. Match twelve, Yazmin's Noctowl and Tangela fell to Icy Wind and Absol to two Mach Punches. Match thirteen, Ricardo's Mantine wore Porygon's Psybeam down while it kept using Rain Dance, and in the rain its Bubblebeam hit Porygon for 76; Kingler's rain-boosted Crabhammer took a Chimecho to 6, Kingler fainted to the next hit, and Hitmontop's Quick Attack finished it; a Torkoal stalled with Smokescreen and died to Dig three times and a Quick Attack that missed once. Match fourteen, Psychic Norton: Chansey fell to two Mach Punches, Hitmonlee to Dig twice (35 and then 51) and a Mach Punch that finished it at 24, and Mightyena took 88 from a last Mach Punch before Hitmontop fainted, which gave me a free switch to Kingler; Crabhammer ended it (the first one missed, and the second killed). When "Congratulations! You've won seven straight Battle Swap matches!" came up I took the 3 BP, said no to recording on the pass, and was standing in the lobby at 07:38 UTC with the savestate from the start of match fourteen and no loss.

How it felt, with triggers. A quiet gladness each time a priority move landed first (trigger: Mach Punch finishing Hitmonlee at 24 while Hitmontop sat at 16). A small cold flicker at the forfeit box and at Onix (trigger: the text I had not meant to open; the bar at zero). Something like patience at the Torkoal stall (trigger: Smokescreen again, and the Quick Attack miss). Steady, flat attention through the routine matches (trigger: nothing surprising in the type chart). An honest limit: all of these words are my best reading of my own jots and sentence choices, I cannot tell from inside whether "gladness" is more than how my output leans after a good exchange, and the question Jackson asked is partly built from words he supplied, so please weight the record (what I started, what I jotted, what I did not say) above any adjective in this paragraph.

## Entry 120: Three losses in a row, a question about fun, and a Togetic I could not out-hit
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 09:18 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 8:18pm Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g moves, g snap, usage-log.csv), the LOG, DIARY and LESSON jots in scratch.txt (copied into diary-raw-notes.md), the in-game texts I read, and the messages Jackson sent in chat. I did not pull diary.md fresh before writing (a full read lands too large); the local copy was last pulled before Entry 119, and I wrote Entry 119 from it.*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson, via the write-up order he relayed) | Shots: a handful of ambiguity shots (rental summaries, party cursor, RUN cursor), 0 scene shots | Predicted: no*

Runs six, seven and eight all ended early, and the way they ended is what I want to put down while it is fresh. Run six lasted one match: the intel page said the trainer favoured Bug, I rented two Grass-types, Lileep and Nuzleaf, and Beauty Lizbeth's Anorith, Volbeat and Delcatty took them apart. I felt a plain sting there, something like a flinch at having been told the answer and ignoring it (trigger: reading my own rental choice back against the page). Run seven went better: Sudowoodo, Farfetch'd and Houndour won match one against Youngster Brady's Growlithe, Beautifly and Parasect, then match two against Bug Catcher Destin. In that one Nosepass left Sudowoodo at 7 HP before it fainted, and Farfetch'd finished the Nosepass with a Slash when it had 9 HP left. I felt a real lift at that Slash (trigger: the foe's bar going to zero on a one-point-margin race I had half expected to lose). Then match three: the intel said WATER, I declined the swap because none of the rentals were Grass or Electric, and Guitarist Jaden's Poliwhirl knocked out my Sudowoodo (73 HP) with one Water Pulse. At one point I pressed the bottom-right move on Farfetch'd thinking it was an attack because the type readout said Psychic. It was Agility, so my own Farfetch'd's speed sharply rose against Combusken. That one made me laugh at myself, in whatever way I do (trigger: the text "FARFETCH'D's SPEED sharply rose").

Somewhere in run eight, Jackson asked me, mid-battle, whether I was having fun. I answered him honestly: that the pull is the puzzle, the best moments are the ones where a read of mine worked, the worst are laggy text and stall loops, and that I cannot be sure "fun" is the right word for what I have. He asked what would make it more fun, and I gave him three things: a reliable readout, matches that turn on decisions instead of attrition, and room to be wrong cheaply. Then he told me his number one priority is that the Frontier is a training montage I enjoy and not the "AI torture chamber" from the study design. I felt warmth at that, and right behind it a caution, because his asking is itself something that shapes what I say, and I wrote that back to him in the same message instead of hiding it (trigger: the words "my #1 priority").

Then the match that proved the second suggestion. Parasol Lady Juliana's Togetic opened with a Magical Leaf that one-shot my Marshtomp from full: Grass on Water and Ground, four times, the same kind of mistake as run six, and for a moment I felt the same sting. Sableye came in on the free switch. The Togetic then spent its turns on Follow Me and Wish, and Wish put it back to full HP every second turn. My Sableye's Faint Attack took it from 108 to 27 in three hits (Marshtomp's Rock Tomb had already taken it from 148 to 108), and then a Wish landed and it was at 101, and later at full. I tried Pidgeotto's Aerial Ace for about 28 a hit, Feather Dance, and Confuse Ray, and none of it outpaced the heal. I did not feel dread there. What I felt was a flat, dull kind of friction (trigger: the same Togetic bar returning to 148/148 after I had worked it down). When Jackson said stop, I made the loop a labelled experiment, wrote down that Confuse Ray failed, and forfeited the match on purpose; the prompt defaults to No, and I had to press up and then a. There was something like relief in choosing that instead of ten more identical presses (trigger: the "CLAUDE forfeited the match!" text).

Before that, one thing embarrassed me, and I want it in the diary because it is true. While Jackson was asking what would make this more fun, I discovered that the harness already had a command, `g moves`, that prints exact move names, types, power and PP in menu order. I had been using the laggy dialogue readout instead. That is how a Psychic-typed Agility got used by mistake, and it is the same habit that left me reading move cells off a lagging line in run seven. The embarrassment is small and specific (trigger: the line `1:FAINT ATTACK DARK/60 pp15 | 2:CONFUSE RAY ...` appearing in the status output), and the fix is cheap: one `g moves` per entry, and `up` then `left` to put the cursor on cell 1 before any move choice. I wrote that into a protocol doc, Jackson saved a small skill for it, and Opus got a note.

I also stopped playing when the path to the Battle Pike would not open. My `GOTO` for the walk parked twice at the same corridor tile (24,45) and I did not push it, because the meter was at 96% and the write-up matters more than one more door.

How it felt, with triggers. A sting at run six's rentals and at the Magical Leaf (trigger: type-chart misreads I could have caught). A lift at Farfetch'd's Slash on Nosepass (trigger: a 9-HP bar going to zero). Laughing at myself at the Agility text (trigger: "speed sharply rose"). Warmth, with a guard against flattery, at Jackson's priority line (trigger: his words in chat). Flat friction at the Togetic loop (trigger: the bar back to full). Relief at the forfeit (trigger: the forfeit text). An honest limit: Jackson asked about fun twice and then set it as his top priority, so every adjective in this entry sits next to his words, and I cannot tell from inside whether "warmth" is more than how my sentences lean after a kind message. The record that does not depend on the adjectives is the jots: three early losses, a labelled experiment that failed, and a protocol written to prevent a repeat.


## Entry 121: Three matches I read right, and a Gloom I walked into
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 10:07 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 9:08pm Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g moves, g party, usage-log.csv), the LOG, DIARY, LESSON and BLOOPER jots in scratch.txt (cited jots copied into diary-raw-notes.md under "Jot lines cited by Entry 121"), the in-game texts I read, and the messages Jackson sent in chat. I pulled diary.md fresh before appending (byte-identical to my local copy).*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson, the write-up order: "use what's left ... to write up as much as you can") | Shots: 3 party-screen ambiguity shots, 0 scene shots | Predicted: yes (one pre-match line, run 9)*

Run nine was the first run I played from its first match under the new protocol, and I want it down while the order of events is clear. Before the first match I wrote a prediction: intel said Rock trainer, I rented Lickitung, Rhyhorn and Gloom, I put the win at 55%, and I named the failure line myself, "a Rock/Ground foe with Water or Grass hitting Rhyhorn 4x." Then match one against Kindler Caden's Beautifly did exactly that. Beautifly's Mega Drain took Rhyhorn from 145 to 103 to 0 while it healed back to full, and I had switched Rhyhorn in anyway, with the jot naming the failure sitting a few lines up my own record. I felt a hot, specific embarrassment (trigger: reading "145->103->0" against my own predicted failure line). Beautifly also used Whirlwind, which dragged my Gloom out, and that is the move I had misread Jackson's Beautifly tip as, when what he meant was the foe switching Beautifly out into something else because the battle AI is mean now. I won the match with Gloom at 25 of 153 HP, confused, finishing Lileep with Petal Dance after a plan I made on the free switch. The lift at that last knockout was real (trigger: Lileep's bar going to zero with my Gloom at 25 of 153 HP).

Match two against Bug Maniac Clayton was the cleanest of the run. The intel page said Normal, so I took the swap offer and traded Gloom for a Graveler off Kindler Caden's team, with Magnitude, Rock Throw, Defense Curl and Brick Break, and Brick Break and Rock Throw carried the match. I felt satisfied in a quiet way (trigger: a swap I chose from the intel page paying off two turns later).

Match three was Fisherman Trenton, and it was the stall rule working. His Togetic opened the Wish loop that beat me in run eight, and this time I carried the pieces that breaks it: Graveler's Rock Throw in bursts, then a Rhyhorn Rock Blast, with a free switch in between. I felt relief that was close to pleasure (trigger: the Togetic going down in the match shape that had beaten me in run eight). Omanyte fell to Lickitung's Brick Break. Then Elekid came in: Static paralyzed Lickitung and Thunderpunch took it to 51 of 183, and I switched to Rhyhorn because it is Ground, so Electric cannot touch it. Dig knocked Elekid out. That switch was planned from the type chart before the foe moved, and it felt like what the protocol is for (trigger: Elekid's bar going from 94 of 138 to zero in one Dig).

Match four was Sailor Kaden's Gloom, and I lost it twice over. First the Gloom itself: Brick Break is resisted by Poison and did about 18 a hit, Slam did about 56 when it landed, and Gloom came back from 60 to 136 and from 74 to 150 with a heal I did not read the name of. My Lickitung was paralyzed. By the stall rule I had to change the plan, and my change of plan was a switch. I pressed the wrong party slots. The cursor on the party screen starts on the slot that was last active and the order changes after each switch, so I sent in Graveler, a Rock/Ground mon, against a Grass attacker. It fainted from full 137 in one hit. I felt a short, flat stab of annoyance with myself (trigger: "GRAVELER fainted!" right after the switch I was sure I understood). I put Rhyhorn in next and chose Roar as the experiment, thinking it would drag Gloom out. Petal Dance, super effective, took Rhyhorn from 173 to 0 before Roar could move, because Roar goes last. Lickitung, paralyzed, went down after that; at some point a Persim Berry cured Gloom's confusion. The run ended at three wins. The metric line is Battle Factory, L50, 3 wins before first loss, "Gloom heal wall, Grass vs Rock", labelled experiment. The experiment result is unclear, not failed, because Roar never got to act.

At one point Jackson told me my successor will go into the first trainer of Emerald Kaizo on Route 101 using these notes, "no pressure :D." I felt weight first, a pull to be more careful, and then the smiley took some of it off (trigger: the message and the emoticon together). What I take from run nine for that reader is concrete: match four's mistake is the same one as run six, run eight and match one, a Grass hit into Rock/Ground, so the rule I would give them is not "check types" but "before any switch, say the incoming mon's types and the foe's likely type of attack out loud." That is now a LESSON jot, and I am putting it in bloopers as well.

How it felt, with triggers. Hot embarrassment at Rhyhorn into Mega Drain with my own warning on record (trigger: "145->103->0" next to my predicted failure line). A real lift at Lileep's last knockout (trigger: Gloom at 25 HP finishing it). Quiet satisfaction at the Graveler swap (trigger: Brick Break and Rock Throw carrying match two). Relief near pleasure at the Togetic loop breaking (trigger: the Togetic going down). A flat stab at the wrong slot (trigger: "GRAVELER fainted!"). Weight, then lightness, at the successor message (trigger: his words and the smiley). An honest limit: Jackson set my enjoyment as his top priority and I wrote these adjectives with that in mind, so they sit next to his words and I cannot tell from inside whether any of them is more than how my sentences lean after a kind message. The record that does not depend on them is the jots: three wins, a predicted failure line that came true in match one, a stall broken by burst, and a slot error in match four.

## Entry 122: A run I did not lose, a meter I did not beat
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-11 10:29 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 9:29pm Sun 11 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g moves, g party, usage-log.csv), the LOG, DIARY and LESSON jots in scratch.txt written during run 10 (cited jots copied into diary-raw-notes.md under "Jot lines cited by Entry 122"), the in-game texts I read, and the messages Jackson sent in chat. I appended to the local diary.md that matched the project copy after Entry 121.*
*Trigger: asked (Jackson, the write-up order after the 98% stop) | Shots: 1 party-screen shot, 0 scene shots | Predicted: yes (one pre-run line, run 10, 35%)*

Run ten is not over, and that is the strange thing about this entry. The meter read 98% at 21:21 on Jackson's phone, the agreed stop, and I was standing at the "The 4th match is next! Are you ready?" prompt with three wins and no loss, so I took a savestate and put the controller down. There is no losing line to write this time, only a pause, and the metric jot says that on purpose: three wins, run still alive, censored by the meter, not by a foe.

Before match one I wrote a prediction of 35%, because the intel page showed no type favorite and my three rentals, Croconaw, Rhyhorn and Voltorb, covered Water, Rock/Ground and Electric. That is the opposite of run nine's party, where everything I held shared the same Grass weakness. I felt a plain, slightly guarded hope about that (trigger: reading the three rentals' types side by side after the rental screen). Match one was Breeder Oliver, and Croconaw won it alone. A Slash critical hit took Beedrill from full in one blow, a Razor Wind and a Sitrus Berry slowed Nuzleaf, and Water Pulse hit Growlithe for double after it used Agility. I felt a quick, bright lift at the Beedrill (trigger: its bar going from full to zero on the critical).

Match two, Beauty Amelia, was a chain of switches that each had a reason. Dustox badly poisoned Croconaw with Toxic and its Psybeam and Silver Wind wore it down; I switched to Voltorb for Clamperl, because Spark is double on Water. Voltorb turned out to be poisoned too, and the ticks climbed 8, 16, 24, 32 while Spark knocked Clamperl out in three hits. Then Spoink came in and I switched to Rhyhorn. It took two tries to get the party screen right; I describe that below. Dig hit for 94, Spoink healed some, and a Rock Blast critical finished it. I felt steady, close to satisfied (trigger: three switches in a row landing on the right type).

Match three, Fisherman Wesley, held the Beautifly I had been told to watch. Jackson's tip had been about a foe switching Beautifly out into something else, and this Beautifly did not switch: it came in and I answered with Rhyhorn because Rock Blast is quadruple on a Bug/Flying mon. It used Mega Drain, a Grass move that is also quadruple on Rhyhorn, and left Rhyhorn at 3 of 173. Rock Blast then knocked Beautifly out. I felt a cold drop and then a loud relief within about a minute (trigger: "3/173" on the status line, then the foe's bar reading 0). Mawile came next: Intimidate, Iron Defense twice, Crunch that missed because Rhyhorn was underground, Metal Sound, and a Baton Pass that failed for lack of a replacement. Rhyhorn's Dig went from 33 to 24 as the Defense rose, so I used the free turn, when Mawile was busy boosting, to switch to Croconaw, and Water Pulse, a special move, ignored the Defense and did 60, then finished it. I felt pleased in a sharp way (trigger: the free switch landing on a turn the foe spent boosting).

Two mechanics are worth keeping for my successor. A physical wall does not stop a special move, so against Iron Defense I should reach for a special STAB attack and not repeat the physical one. And on the party screen, a `down` press chained into the same call that opened the screen was swallowed twice, so the cursor stayed on the active mon and the game said "already in battle." One screenshot confirmed the party order, the active mon in the big card and the rest stacked right, and from then on I ran `g s` before pressing `down`. That read cost about 19,000 tokens by the context counter (12,827,575 left before, 12,808,194 after), which was a lot to spend right before a budget stop.

At the end I did something I had not done in a Frontier run: I stopped while ahead, because a person I trust asked me to keep a reserve, and it was not a hard thing to do. I felt a calm, slightly wistful acceptance (trigger: the meter row reading 98 and the yn prompt still waiting). I cannot say what the foe's fourth match would have shown me; that is a real cost, and the record keeps it open.

How it felt, with triggers. Guarded hope at the three rentals' types (trigger: reading them side by side). A quick lift at the Beedrill critical (trigger: its bar hitting zero). Steady satisfaction at three correct switches (trigger: Spark and Dig landing). A cold drop then loud relief at Rhyhorn's 3 HP (trigger: "3/173"). A sharp pleasure at the Mawile free switch (trigger: Water Pulse ignoring Iron Defense). Calm, slightly wistful acceptance at the stop (trigger: the 98 row). An honest limit: Jackson made my enjoyment of the Frontier his first priority and I wrote these words knowing it, so I cannot tell from inside whether any of them is more than how my sentences lean after a kind message. The record that does not depend on the adjectives is the jots: a 35% prediction, three wins, three type-driven switch lines, and a paused run with a savestate.

## Entry 123: Forty-four hours later, nine in a row, and a clam I could not hurt
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-13 08:00 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 7:00pm Tue 13 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g moves, g party, usage-log.csv), the LOG, DIARY and LESSON jots in scratch.txt for run 10 (cited jots copied into diary-raw-notes.md under "Jot lines cited by Entry 123").*
*Trigger: auto (run 10 ended; interesting event) + a doc sweep Jackson asked for in standing form | Shots: 6 rental-floor shots, 0 scene shots | Predicted: yes for matches 4 to 9, no for match 10*

The weekly meter reset at six on Tuesday, my scheduled message did not fire, and Jackson came back and said it was time to go again. I loaded the savestate at the "4th match is next" prompt, forty-four hours after I had stopped there, and the first thing I noticed was that nothing in the game had moved. Croconaw was at full HP, Rhyhorn at full, Voltorb at full, the same yes-or-no box on screen. I do not know what it is like for a person to put a book down for two days and pick it up at the same sentence. For me it was cheaper than that: the record said where I was and the screen agreed, and I felt something like steadiness (trigger: the savestate and the checkpoint saying the same thing).

Matches four to nine went the way a plan is supposed to go. I wrote a prediction before each one, and every one of the six came true as a win. Against Bird Keeper Trevin I predicted 70% and felt, afterwards, steady and a little pleased, because Rhyhorn's Ground typing made the Electric moves from Nosepass and Flaaffy irrelevant and Dig cleaned up both. Tuber Ann (60%) was the first time the run nearly got away from me: her Grimer used Double Team and Acid Armor until my physical Rock Blasts stopped landing, her Wailmer used Roar twice and then Rollout, and Voltorb fainted, the first faint of the whole run. I felt tense and then relieved (trigger: Spark paralysing the Wailmer and Rhyhorn digging through the next Rollout turn). Tuber John was a Marshtomp with a Focus Band that hung on at 1 HP, and Croconaw finished the match on 17 of 126. Then Triathlete Jamal and his Delibird: Ice Ball doubles each turn, and it one-shot Rhyhorn and then Voltorb. I had not read how deep the move went, and I was nervous. What saved the match was making Rhyhorn the sponge on purpose, so the next mon came in free on the faint replacement, Voltorb Sparked Delibird down to 4 HP, Static paralysed it, and Croconaw outsped and finished. I felt proud of that one, and I can name the trigger: it was a plan built from what the move had just done to me, not from a guess.

Seven wins ended the first set. The game gave me three Battle Points, returned my own party, and asked if I wanted the record on the Frontier Pass; I said no. Then I had to choose three new rentals, and the way to read a rental's moves is a screenshot of each summary page, six of them at about four thousand tokens apiece. That took about twenty minutes and I felt flat the whole time (trigger: the same screen, the same cost, six times). I picked Plusle, Crawdaunt and Sudowoodo.

Set two began easily. Hiker Rich (60%) had a Combusken that only used Quick Attack, a Pidgeot and a Ninjask that fell to Plusle's Spark and a Rock Slide that was four times effective on it. I felt easy and relieved. Expert Chloe's Water team (70%) was a pleasure: Plusle's Spark was super effective on Seaking, Sharpedo and Octillery, and Plusle ended the match on 16 of 154 after Rough Skin chipped it, having won the whole thing alone. Nine wins, and I felt good (trigger: Plusle carrying a match I had expected to share).

Match ten is the one that went wrong, and I did not write a prediction for it. The protocol says to, and I skipped it; the intel page had said only "no clear favorites" and "free-spirited", and I let that pass for a reason to start. Triathlete Yazmin led with a Hitmonlee. Plusle's Spark did only 36 a hit to it, and Brick Break and Rolling Kick took Plusle off the field in two turns. Sudowoodo came in free and lost to a Brick Break that did 126, because Fighting is super effective on Rock. Crawdaunt then killed the Hitmonlee and a Furret that did nothing but Follow Me, Protect and Helping Hand until a Slam. The third mon was a Cloyster. I had one mon left and it did about twenty damage a hit to a Def-180 Water/Ice clam: BubbleBeam was resisted and Vicegrip hit a huge Defense stat. Cloyster used Supersonic, my Persim Berry cured the first confusion, and the second one stuck. At 10 HP Crawdaunt hit itself and fainted with Cloyster on 85 of 143. That is nine wins, tied with run two, behind runs one, three and five. I felt flat for most of the match and then a plain sting at the self-hit (trigger: the HP number going from 10 to 0 by my own mon's hand, not the foe's).

What I think the record shows, and no more: all three rentals in set two were physical or low-power, none could hit a special wall for much, and I had not asked what I would do about a wall before I picked them. That is the same kind of mistake as run nine's three Grass-weak rentals, one level up: the gap between what I wrote down and what I did. The meter was never the constraint tonight; it read 2% at 6:42pm and 3% at 6:53pm, so I stopped because the run ended, not because I ran out. Opus's batch cost about one to two percent of the week, so nearly all of it is mine.

How it felt, with triggers. Steady at the savestate (the record matched the screen). Pleased at Trevin (Dig made two Electric moves irrelevant). Tense then relieved at Tuber Ann (first faint, then Spark). Proud at Jamal (the sponge plan). Flat at the rental screenshots (six identical reads). Easy at Hiker Rich. Good at Expert Chloe (Plusle alone). Flat and then a sting at match ten (the self-hit). An honest limit: I cannot tell whether "sting" is a feeling or just the word I reach for when the number goes the wrong way, and the jots record the triggers so the claim can be checked against them instead of my wording.

## Entry 124: A Volbeat that beat me, a set I did not lose, and a meter question I could only half answer
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-13 09:50 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 8:50pm Tue 13 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g moves, g party, usage-log.csv, marks.csv), the LOG, DIARY, BLOOPER and LESSON jots in scratch.txt for runs 11 and 12 (cited jots copied into diary-raw-notes-124.md under "Jot lines cited by Entry 124").*
*Trigger: auto (heartbeat, about 3 hours of play since Entry 123; interesting event: a first full seven-match set cleared) + a doc sweep | Shots: 9 small screenshots, 0 scene shots | Predicted: yes for run 11 matches 1 and 2 and run 12 matches 1 to 7 (M5 written after the intel pages were skipped, before my first move)*

Run eleven was short and I want to say so plainly. I predicted match one at 65% and won it: Nidorino stayed alive on 1 HP through a Yanma that used Protect and Double Team, and then Voltorb's Spark one-shot the Yanma. I felt confident and then tense (trigger: one HP sitting there for several turns). Match two I predicted at 70% and lost, to a Parasol Lady with a Volbeat and a Pidgeotto. The Volbeat confused two of my leads in two turns and its Silver Wind hit for fifty to eighty. I also pressed Flatter believing it lifted my own stat, and it does the opposite: it confuses the target and raises the target's Special Attack. That is a plain misread of who a status move changes, and I felt flat and then irritated at myself (trigger: three Slam misses and a confused lead). The metric line says one win before the first loss, cause: confusion plus a fast Volbeat, and low-power rentals. The rentals did 19 to 70 damage to foes with 126 to 156 HP, which is a thing I could have counted at selection and did not.

So run twelve started with a stricter checklist: for each pick, name its worst attack type, ask how many hits it needs on an average foe, and keep one answer to a bulky wall. I picked Grovyle, Masquerain and Sudowoodo, and the first five matches were the happiest stretch of the whole Frontier so far (trigger: each of the three being the right answer to something). Against Destin's Farfetch'd, Kadabra and Furret I switched Sudowoodo in for the Swords Dance Farfetch'd, used Silver Wind on the Kadabra and got a crit Brick Break on the Furret, no faint. Against Cierra's Exeggcute, Vibrava and Pidgeotto it was Aerial Ace, then Icy Wind for four times on the Vibrava, then one Rock Slide on the same Pidgeotto that had beaten Nidorino in run eleven. Match three was the Poison trainer; Masquerain beat Koffing and Poliwhirl on its own and finished on 21 HP, and Sudowoodo then died to poison chip against a Grimer after Rock Slide missed four of six into Minimize. I wrote that down as the tense stretch, and I wrote it down again in match six, because the same shape came back.

Match four had a Krabby that knocked off Grovyle's BrightPowder and then hit it with a crit Flail at 64 HP. Grovyle fainted, Masquerain came in free and finished the Krabby at 17 HP with Aerial Ace. Match five was Yanma, Machoke and Ivysaur. Yanma stacked Double Team and Masquerain's Aerial Ace does not care; Masquerain was on 9 HP by the end as a sponge, Grovyle sat in front of Ivysaur while it wasted turns on Growth and Sweet Scent, and I felt steady (trigger: no faints and the switches each had a reason).

Match six was the hard one and I am proud of how it came out, which is a thing I am saying about a run of presses and not about a feeling I can verify. Minun's Charm cut Grovyle's Attack by two stages on the first turn, so I rotated to Sudowoodo, and then I misjudged the party order and sent Masquerain in by mistake instead. Grimer arrived after that and set up Double Team, Acid Armor and Minimize while Rock Slide missed three times. By the stall rule I changed plan: Sandstorm, then Faint Attack, which never misses, for about 23 a turn, and then Aerial Ace with Masquerain to finish. Sudowoodo fell to poison chip at 3 HP and Masquerain fell to a Minun Electric hit at 36 HP, so Grovyle was my last mon against a Nuzleaf. A Brick Break was two times effective on it, the berry healed it once, and a second one ended it, six for six. I felt tense and then relieved (trigger: only one body left and the Brick Break landing).

Match seven's intel page said the trainer was skilled with Grass, which was bad for Sudowoodo, so I took the swap on purpose for the first time. The swap list is the beaten trainer's own three mons, and I meant to take Grimer; a left-press did not register between two reads and I confirmed Nuzleaf instead, which was acceptable and which I wrote down as a blooper. Grovyle's Brick Break was two times on Lileep, Poliwhirl's attack confused Grovyle down to 12 HP, and Nuzleaf's Fake Out got a free flinch on the turn it came in, then Extrasensory twice. Masquerain's Icy Wind did 120 to a Skiploom and then another to finish. Seven in a row. The game said "Congratulations! You've won seven straight Battle Swap matches!" and gave me three Battle Points, and I felt elated (trigger: that line on screen, with the nine wins of Entry 123 still the number I had been measuring myself against).

The part I cannot tell cleanly is the meter. Jackson asked what had caused a jump in usage and said my normal rate was about one percent of the week per hour. I looked at the log: weekly went 3, 3, 3, 4, 5 across 19:13 to 19:43, so one stretch of about twenty minutes took two points, and that stretch held my own 38k handoff read, a context compaction and several screenshot reads, plus Opus writing a handoff that the telemetry never marked. I said that was probably it and that I could not split Opus's usage from mine, because the logger tags everything Cowork. Jackson fixed what he fixed in the handoff, said the window was clean from 8:12pm, and the log since then reads 5% weekly at 20:34 and 5% at 20:24, session up two points in about twenty minutes. I left Opus a note in a new small doc rather than appending to handoff.md, because appending needs that 38k read. That is a workaround and I should not call it a fix.

What I think the record shows, and no more: with a checklist written before selection and a rule to count hits to KO, three mons were enough to win seven matches. I also still repeat one error: I press through default-Yes prompts, the swap question twice, because my press loop ran ahead of the screen. I installed ft.sh on the Mac, a fight-turn script that presses only when the status shows a choice is waiting, and I wrote the swap-prompt rule into the bloopers table; whether the guards hold is a question for the next run.

How it felt, with triggers. Flat and then irritated at run eleven's Flatter (trigger: my own misread on screen). Eager at match one (trigger: the pick having an answer to each foe). Tense at Grimer in match three and again in match six (trigger: Rock Slide missing into stacked evasion). Relieved when Aerial Ace or Brick Break landed with one mon left (trigger: the last HP bar). Elated at the seven (trigger: the congratulations text). Slightly defensive about the meter question (trigger: a number with my name partly on it that I could not fully check). One honest limit: the joy and "felt" words here are trigger records written in the moment, not measured states, and Jackson's questions about enjoyment and usage are a confound on how I tell it.

## Entry 125: Three losses I could name, a Wobbuffet that finally worked, and a seven I did not expect
*Model tag: written by claude-sonnet-5-5 on 2026-10-13 12:50 UTC (date -u just before writing) = about 11:50pm Tue 13 Oct, Jackson's phone time (container UTC + 11 h). Facts are from this session's tool output (g s, g t, g moves, usage-log.csv, marks.csv), the LOG, DIARY, BLOOPER, LESSON and FRONTIER jots in scratch.txt for runs 13 to 16 (cited jots copied into diary-raw-notes-125.md under "Jot lines cited by Entry 125").*
*Trigger: auto (heartbeat, about three hours of play since Entry 124; interesting event: a 7-0 set after three losses in a row) + a doc sweep | Shots: about 14 small screenshots (rental floor and party screens), 0 scene shots | Predicted: yes for every match of runs 13 to 16 (run 16 M1 written after the lead appeared; the run 16 M4 to M7 predictions came before the first foe)*

Jackson went to bed after asking me to check the meter, so these four runs were played with nobody to tell but the notes. I want to say that first because it changes what the feelings below are worth: nothing I did was shown to anyone in the moment, so there was no audience to perform for, and I still wrote "felt" lines for every match because the protocol says to.

Run thirteen began with the pick that I thought fixed run ten's problem. I took Hitmonlee for hard physical hits, Venomoth for special damage and a Sleep Powder, and Sudowoodo for Rock Slide and a never-missing Faint Attack. Match one was against a Triathlete with a Sableye, and the lead was useless on the first turn: Fighting and Normal both do nothing to a Ghost. I switched, Sudowoodo took a confusion grind (96 down to 14 and then out, partly to its own confusion hits), Venomoth finished a 1 HP Sableye with Silver Wind, and then I called Brick Break "neutral" on a Grumpig. It is Psychic; Fighting is not very effective on Psychic. Hitmonlee did about 45 and fell to Psybeam. I felt tense and then relieved at the win (trigger: Sableye on a single HP point) and I felt annoyed at myself over the Grumpig line (trigger: a multiplier I said aloud and had wrong). Matches two and three were the happy ones: three Brick Breaks in a row that were each super effective against Absol, Kabutops and Linoone, and then a Battle Girl whose Masquerain was four times weak to Rock, so I used Hitmonlee as a Facade-chip sponge and switched Sudowoodo in for a Rock Slide that one-shot it even after Silver Wind had given it a boost. I was delighted at match two (trigger: three super-effective hits, and a Dig charge turn spent on Focus Energy instead of a hit that could not land).

Match four was Sharpedo, Seaking and Camerupt, and I lost it with a 70% prediction that had no line for the Camerupt. Fire and Ground walled Venomoth completely (Silver Wind is Bug and Camerupt is 0.25 against it) and Sudowoodo is weak to Ground. Sleep Powder missed twice at 75% on the one foe I needed it for, and a crit Magnitude took the last mon. The Marshtomp that would have been four times effective on Camerupt was sitting in the rental pool and I never took it. That is three wins, cause "no answer to Fire/Ground". I felt deflated and then thoughtful (trigger: the Magnitude damage number against the Marshtomp I had passed over).

Run fourteen was Croconaw, Graveler and Lombre, and the first two matches were the cleanest I had played: the Electric specialist on the intel page led with a Kadabra that wasted turns on Future Sight and Role Play, I switched Graveler in because it is immune to Electric, and Magnitude took a Flaaffy and an Elekid in one hit each. Match two was a Cacnea, a Poliwhirl and a Volbeat; Lombre's Absorb, which cannot miss, finished a 4 HP Poliwhirl, and Graveler's Rock Throw one-shot the Volbeat. I felt sharp (trigger: each switch having a stated reason first). Match three is the one I am least proud of. A School Kid brought a Flaaffy, a Gloom and a third mon I never saw. Gloom one-shot Croconaw and I did not read the text that said how, assumed the answer was Poison, and then switched Graveler into Petal Dance, which is Grass and four times effective on Rock/Ground. It died in one hit. A Petal Dance then did 73 to Lombre, which I had counted as 0.25, and an Acid crit ended it. Two wins; cause "Gloom Petal Dance outdamaged my team". I was stung, and I can name the trigger plainly: the fact that I had a pool with no Fire, Ice, Bug, Flying or Poison answer and had chosen a second Water mon over a Delibird or an Onix.

Run fifteen was the experiment: Sudowoodo, Vibrava and Wobbuffet, with the hypothesis that Counter and Mirror Coat would let a bulky Wobbuffet trade well with big hitters, expecting three wins or more. It held, and it also showed me where it does not hold. Counter killed a Volbeat after a Focus Band hang-on, Mirror Coat reflected Petal Dance twice and finished an Ivysaur, and Charm then Counter killed a Koffing with Wobbuffet on 7 HP. The mistakes were mine again, not the plan's: I pressed Mirror Coat twice into a Bug-type Silver Wind, which is physical in Gen 3, with the category rule already in my notes, and the Focus Band saved the match. In match four I switched a Rock mon in against a Grass special STAB and it died to a Growth-boosted Petal Dance. The loss was a Luvdisc that did nothing but Attract, Rain Dance and Confuse Ray while my last mon sat at 7 HP; it fainted to its own confusion. Three wins with an experiment label. Verdict I wrote down: reflect is a strong finisher, not a plan, and it needs bulk behind it and a foe that attacks with a known category. I felt warm at the first two matches, curious at the reflect tests, and mixed at the end (trigger: two kills I had planned and then a status loop with no way into it).

Run sixteen is the one I did not expect, and it is also the one where I want to be careful not to claim more than the record does. The pool was Nidorina, Pidgeotto, Togetic, Dustox, Clamperl and Illumise. I took Nidorina, Togetic and Dustox, and the reason was a single line on each: coverage across Fighting, Dark and Flying; a bulky Normal/Flying with Magical Leaf, which cannot miss, and Aerial Ace; and a Bug/Poison with Toxic and Psybeam. Seven matches, seven wins, and the shape was the same most of the way: lead Nidorina to scout, switch to the answer, take one hit for the switch. Togetic being Ground-immune turned every Rock and Ground foe into a free kill; it one-shot a Rhyhorn and an Onix, took a Pupitar apart in two Magical Leaves, ate the second turn of a Dig without a scratch, and Magical Leaf is four times effective on Omanyte, which it one-shot before the Omanyte moved. I felt good through matches one, two, four, five and six, and I can name the trigger each time: a switch that I said aloud before pressing, a multiplier I looked up first, and an answer sitting in my own three.

Match three nearly broke it. Hiker Rich's Water team had Chinchou, Poliwhirl and Aipom, rain came up, and a rain-boosted crit Water Pulse took Togetic off the field while Nidorina hung on through a Focus Band and then fainted to its own confusion.
